349	## Goal2: per-actor threads + mpsc channels (0.1.0-7)
350	
351	Implements Goal2 of Stage 1: two actors running on their own
352	threads, ping-ponging an empty [`Message`] over `std::sync::mpsc`
353	channels, with the same warmup/measurement/calibration lifecycle
354	as goal1.
355	
356	### Design decisions
357	
358	- **Factory closures instead of pre-boxed actors.** `add_actor`
359	  takes `FnOnce() -> A` where `A: Actor + Send + 'static`; the
360	  factory runs inside the actor's own thread. Keeps the [`Actor`]
361	  trait free of a `Send` bound and matches how real actor systems
362	  typically spawn state.
363	- **In-band shutdown via internal `Signal` enum.** Channel payload
364	  is `Signal { User(Message), ClearProbe, Shutdown }`, not
365	  `Message` directly. Main thread injects `ClearProbe` at the
366	  warmup → measurement boundary and `Shutdown` after measurement;
367	  actors match on the variant in their `recv()` loop. Avoids an
368	  out-of-band atomic flag and the associated polling.
369	- **`inner` fixed at 1.** Ping-pong keeps at most one message in
370	  flight per channel, so batched probe scopes (`inner > 1`) could
371	  never fill — every scope would hit the partial-batch drop path.
372	  The `--inner` flag from goal1 is not exposed; the hardcoded
373	  `inner=1` is reflected in the throughput line.
374	- **Per-actor probes, merged reporting.** Each actor thread owns
375	  a [`TProbe2`] named `"goal2.dispatch.actor{id}"`. Main collects
376	  `(count, probe)` on join and prints a per-actor band table (not
377	  merged — two independent distributions are more informative
378	  than one blended histogram in a ping-pong workload where the
379	  actors should be near-identical).
380	- **Calibration pre-spawn on main thread.** Matches goal1's
381	  calibration code path. Same CPU → same `Overhead` applies to
382	  all actor threads. Could be refined later to calibrate
383	  per-thread, but unnecessary for Stage 1.
384	
385	### File-level edits
386	
387	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-7.
388	- `src/runtime.rs`: add `MultiThreadRuntime`, `Signal` enum,
389	  `ActorFactory` alias, `MultiCtx`, and `actor_loop` helper below
390	  the existing single-thread code. New `#[cfg(test)] mod mt_tests`
391	  with a ping-pong smoke test (50 ms warmup + 50 ms measurement).
392	- `src/bin/goal2.rs`: new binary mirroring goal1's CLI shape
393	  (version banner, help width cap, --warmup, -t/--ticks, --raw),
394	  no --inner. Constructs `MultiThreadRuntime`, adds two
395	  `PingPongActor` factories, seeds one message, calls
396	  `rt.run(warmup, measurement)`, prints summary + apparatus +
397	  per-actor reports.
398	
399	### Smoke (release, `--warmup 0`)
400	
401	- `goal2 0.5` — 116,495 messages in 0.5 s (~0.23 M msg/s).
402	  Mean per-dispatch ~1.17 µs; median ~1.34 µs; p99 tail to
403	  ~22 µs (mostly scheduling jitter). Actors near-symmetric
404	  (58,247 vs 58,248 messages).
405	- `goal2 -h` — wraps at 80 cols, shows the `actor-x1 0.1.0-7`
406	  banner, no `--inner` flag.
407	- `goal2 0.2 --raw` — apparatus: `raw (no overhead subtraction)`;
408	  calibration skipped.
409	- goal1 unchanged: still ~200 M msg/s at `--inner 1000`.
410	- Tests: 15/15 pass (adds `multi_thread_runs_measures_and_shuts_down_cleanly`).
411	
412	### The ~250× single-thread gap
413	
414	Goal1 at `inner=1000` hits ~205 M msg/s (~5 ns/dispatch);
415	goal2 at `inner=1` hits ~0.23 M msg/s (~4 µs/round-trip, ~1.2 µs
416	per-event inside the probe scope). The bot thinks the gap is
417	dominated by: (1) mpsc's `Mutex` + condvar wake for every
418	message, (2) two cross-thread context switches per ping-pong
419	round, (3) cache-line bouncing between the two actors' threads.
420	An unbounded channel or a lock-free mpsc implementation would
421	close some of this but the context-switch floor is fundamental.
422	
423	## `--pin` for thread CPU affinity (0.1.0-8)
424	
425	Adds `--pin <CORES>` to both binaries. Tightens the stdev of
426	probe reports by eliminating OS thread-migration noise, at the
427	cost of exposing placement sensitivity in goal2 (pinning to
428	cross-CCX cores raises the mean because every message forces a
429	cross-complex cache coherence round-trip).
430	
431	Adapted from `../iiac-perf/src/pin.rs` with only the two
432	primitives we need (`parse_cores`, `pin_current`); the affinity
433	save/restore, `--no-pin-cal`, and mask-summary helpers are
434	elided — Stage 1 doesn't need them.
435	
436	### File-level edits
437	
438	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-8; add `core_affinity = "0.8"`.
439	- `src/perf/pin.rs`: new module. `parse_cores(&str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String>`
440	  (comma-separated / range list, duplicates preserved for
441	  oversubscription) and `pin_current(Option<usize>)` (pin the
442	  calling thread; no-op on `None`). 7 unit tests for the parser
443	  (plain list, range, mixed, duplicates, empty string, reverse
444	  range errors, garbage errors).
445	- `src/perf/mod.rs`: `pub mod pin`.
446	- `src/runtime.rs`: `MultiThreadRuntime::run` gains a
447	  `pin_cores: &[usize]` parameter; actor `i` pins to
448	  `pin_cores[i % pin_cores.len()]` inside its spawned thread
449	  (before the actor factory runs or the probe is created). Empty
450	  slice = unpinned (existing behavior). The lone mt_test updated
451	  to pass `&[]`.
452	- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: `--pin <CORES>` flag (`Option<String>`;
453	  parsed via `pin::parse_cores` in `main`). Uses the first core
454	  of the list (goal1 is single-threaded); extra cores silently
455	  ignored. Pins the main thread before calibration and dispatch.
456	  New `pinning: …` line in the output.
457	- `src/bin/goal2.rs`: `--pin <CORES>` flag, same parsing. Parsed
458	  list passed through to `rt.run(warmup, measurement, &pins)`.
459	  `pinning: actor0→coreN, actor1→coreM, …` line in the output.
460	
461	### A/B on this box (3900X, 12-core / 24-thread)
462	
463	`goal2 0.5 --warmup 0.5` — same workload, three placements:
464	
465	| Pinning     | Throughput | Mean / event | Mean min-p99 | Stdev min-p99 |
466	|---          |---         |---           |---           |---            |
467	| unpinned    | 0.374 M/s  | 632 ns       | 622 ns       | **246 ns**    |
468	| `--pin 0,1` | 0.308 M/s  | 667 ns       | 654 ns       | 171 ns        |
469	| `--pin 0,4` | 0.212 M/s  | 1,496 ns     | 1,487 ns     | **80 ns**     |
470	
471	**Stdev tightens dramatically** with pinning: min-p99 stdev
472	drops 246 → 80 ns (3×) at `--pin 0,4`. Migration noise was a
473	real component of the unpinned variance.
474	
475	**p10–p40 rises** (user's prediction): `--pin 0,4` puts the two
476	actors on different CCX complexes (each CCX = 6 cores on Zen 2),
477	so every ping-pong round-trip incurs a cross-CCX cache coherence
478	transfer. Mean climbs from 632 ns → 1,496 ns — the placement cost.
479	`--pin 0,1` (same CCX) stays close to unpinned throughput
480	(~0.31 M/s vs 0.37) but still narrows stdev somewhat.
481	
482	The bot thinks these numbers are the intended story: pinning
483	trades a potentially higher mean for much more reproducible
484	measurements, which is the right tradeoff for code-change A/B
485	work. The placement itself then becomes a tunable — users pick
486	cores that match what they want to measure (best-case
487	same-complex, worst-case cross-socket, etc.).
488	
489	### `goal1 --pin`
490	
491	Single-thread goal1 sees little throughput change with
492	`--pin 5` (~206 M msg/s, identical to unpinned) — all the work
493	is on one thread anyway, and migration cost is already amortized
494	over the 200 M msg/s inner loop. Value of pinning is purely
495	reproducibility.
496

548	## Extract perf into tprobe crate — plan marker (0.2.0-0)
549	
550	Converts the single-crate repo into a Cargo workspace with
551	`actor-x1` and a new `tprobe` crate carved out of `src/perf/`.
552	The 0.1.0-2 chores note flagged "promoting this to a shared
553	crate is worthwhile once Stage 2 is underway" — Stage 1 has
554	shipped and divergence from upstream `iiac-perf` has settled,
555	so this is the natural next step before Stage 2 adds new
556	actors and messages.
557	
558	Multi-step ladder:
559	
560	- `0.2.0-0` — Plan marker. Bump root `Cargo.toml` to `0.2.0-0`;
561	  this chore section; `notes/todo.md` entry under `## In Progress`.
562	  No structural change.
563	- `0.2.0-1` — Workspace layout. Move root `Cargo.toml` + `src/`
564	  into `crates/actor-x1/`. Root `Cargo.toml` becomes a virtual
565	  workspace (`[workspace]` only, `resolver = "2"`,
566	  `members = ["crates/actor-x1", "crates/tprobe"]`). Create
567	  `crates/tprobe/` skeleton (`Cargo.toml` + `src/lib.rs`
568	  docstring stub). `src/perf/*` still lives inside `actor-x1`;
569	  no probe file moves yet. `cargo test` passes; both binaries
570	  behave identically.
571	- `0.2.0-2` — Move perf into tprobe. Move
572	  `crates/actor-x1/src/perf/*` → `crates/tprobe/src/*`;
573	  rewrite internal imports (`crate::perf::x` → `crate::x`).
574	  Migrate `hdrhistogram`, `minstant`, `core_affinity` deps
575	  from actor-x1 to tprobe's `Cargo.toml`. Add
576	  `tprobe = { path = "../tprobe" }` to actor-x1's deps. Drop
577	  `pub mod perf;` from actor-x1's `src/lib.rs`. Rewire call
578	  sites in `runtime.rs`, `goal1.rs`, `goal2.rs`
579	  (`crate::perf::x` → `tprobe::x`). Behavior unchanged; all
580	  tests pass.
581	- `0.2.0-3` — Rename `TProbe2` → `TProbe` and
582	  `tprobe2.rs` → `tprobe.rs`. Pure sweep across the new crate
583	  and its callers. The "2" only communicated "second version
584	  of the probe" inside `iiac-perf`; outside that repo it's
585	  noise. Upstream divergence is already substantial, so the
586	  rename does not meaningfully raise any future re-sync cost.
587	- `0.2.0` — Done marker. Drop `-N` suffix. `notes/todo.md`:
588	  move this task to `## Done`. `notes/chores-01.md`: this
589	  section. `README.md`: update any source-tree or contributor
590	  notes that reference `src/perf/`.
591	
592	### Design decisions recorded here
593	
594	- **Virtual workspace, `crates/` subdir** (option (b) in the
595	  plan discussion): root is `[workspace]`-only, both packages
596	  live under `crates/`. Cleaner symmetric layout than mixing
597	  `[package]` and `[workspace]` at the root, at the cost of a
598	  one-time move of every actor-x1 source file.
599	- **Crate name `tprobe` drops the "2"**. The type rename is
600	  deferred to its own step (`0.2.0-3`) to keep the move diff
601	  (`0.2.0-2`) separate from the rename diff — easier review
602	  for each.
603	- **Perf deps move with the perf code.** `hdrhistogram`,
604	  `minstant`, and `core_affinity` migrate out of actor-x1's
605	  `Cargo.toml` in `0.2.0-2`; actor-x1 picks them up transitively
606	  via `tprobe` without redeclaring.
607	
608	## Workspace layout + tprobe skeleton (0.2.0-1)
609	
610	Converts the repo from a single crate at the root into a virtual
611	Cargo workspace with `actor-x1` and a new `tprobe` crate under
612	`crates/`. No probe code moves yet — `src/perf/*` still lives
613	inside `actor-x1`, and the binaries / tests are unchanged
614	behaviorally. This step only reshuffles the file layout so that
615	`0.2.0-2` can move perf into `tprobe` cleanly.
616	
617	- `Cargo.toml` (new, root): virtual workspace.
618	  `[workspace]` with `resolver = "2"`,
619	  `members = ["crates/actor-x1", "crates/tprobe"]`. No
620	  `[package]` at the root anymore.
621	- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: former root `Cargo.toml`, moved
622	  verbatim; version bumped `0.2.0-0` → `0.2.0-1`. Deps
623	  unchanged (`clap`, `core_affinity`, `hdrhistogram`,
624	  `minstant`) — they migrate to `tprobe` in `0.2.0-2`.
625	- `crates/actor-x1/src/`: former root `src/` moved verbatim.
626	  `lib.rs`, `runtime.rs`, `bin/{goal1,goal2}.rs`, and
627	  `perf/**` all in place; no content edits.
628	- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: new. `name = "tprobe"`,
629	  `version = "0.1.0-0"` (dev-ladder suffix matching the
630	  `actor-x1` convention — drops `-N` when perf moves in at
631	  `0.2.0-2`), same license, empty `[dependencies]`.
632	- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: new. `//!` docstring only;
633	  no public items yet.
634

660	## Move perf into tprobe crate (0.2.0-2)
661	
662	Carries out the body of the `0.2.0` ladder: `src/perf/*` moves
663	from `crates/actor-x1/src/` into `crates/tprobe/src/`; perf
664	dependencies migrate with the code; actor-x1 picks `tprobe` up
665	as a path dependency. Behavior unchanged — tests and binaries
666	run the same workloads with identical output modulo the version
667	banner. The `TProbe2 → TProbe` rename is deliberately deferred
668	to `0.2.0-3`.
669	
670	- `Cargo.lock`: updated by cargo for the new dep graph.
671	- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: version `0.2.0-1` → `0.2.0-2`;
672	  drops `core_affinity`, `hdrhistogram`, `minstant`; adds
673	  `tprobe = { path = "../tprobe" }`.
674	- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: drops `pub mod perf;`.
675	- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs`: `use crate::perf::TProbe2`
676	  → `use tprobe::TProbe2`; `crate::perf::pin::pin_current(...)`
677	  → `tprobe::pin::pin_current(...)`. `cargo fmt` reordered the
678	  import block (local `crate::` before external `tprobe`) per
679	  rustfmt's group-ordering.
680	- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`: `use actor_x1::perf::{self, pin, ticks}`
681	  → `use tprobe::{self as perf, pin, ticks}`. Call sites (e.g.
682	  `perf::calibrate()`) unchanged — the `self as perf` alias
683	  preserves them.
684	- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: same import rewrite as
685	  goal1.
686	- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: version bumped `0.1.0-0` → `0.1.0`
687	  (skeleton → first functional release); adds `core_affinity`,
688	  `hdrhistogram`, `minstant` to `[dependencies]`.
689	- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: rewritten from docstring-only stub
690	  to the full module root — merges the vendored-from-iiac-perf
691	  notes previously in `src/perf/mod.rs`, declares the six
692	  submodules (`band_table`, `fmt`, `overhead`, `pin`, `ticks`,
693	  `tprobe2`), and re-exports `Overhead` / `calibrate` /
694	  `TProbe2` / `TProbe2RecId` at the crate root.
695	- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `overhead.rs`,
696	  `pin.rs`, `ticks.rs`, `ticks/x86_64.rs`, `tprobe2.rs`: moved
697	  verbatim from `crates/actor-x1/src/perf/`. Internal imports
698	  rewritten — every `crate::perf::x` (both `use` lines and
699	  doc-link references) becomes `crate::x` since within `tprobe`
700	  the crate root *is* what `perf/mod.rs` used to be.
701	
702	### Design decisions recorded here
703	
704	- **`tprobe` version bumps `0.1.0-0` → `0.1.0`** at this step
705	  rather than mirroring actor-x1's `-N` ladder. tprobe's own
706	  history is "skeleton → populated", which is a single one-step
707	  release in its own right. Drawing its version clock separately
708	  from actor-x1's matches the "each crate versions itself"
709	  expectation callers have of any Cargo dep.
710	- **`use tprobe::{self as perf, pin, ticks}`** in the binaries
711	  preserves `perf::calibrate()` at the call site. Could have
712	  been rewritten to `tprobe::calibrate()` — chose the alias to
713	  keep the move diff (this step) minimal so `0.2.0-3`'s rename
714	  sweep is the only place the identifier changes. The alias
715	  disappears when the bot thinks the call site should read
716	  `tprobe::…` directly, which is a follow-up worth considering
717	  after the rename lands.
718	- **Imports inside tprobe drop the `perf::` segment** rather
719	  than keeping a nominal `perf` submodule at `crate::perf`. A
720	  standalone crate's root *is* the top of the namespace; adding
721	  an intermediate `perf` module inside `tprobe` would be
722	  redundant and would force every call site to say
723	  `tprobe::perf::x` — worse in every direction.
724	
725	### Verification
726	
727	- `cargo fmt` clean after one small auto-reorg in
728	  `runtime.rs` (see bullet above).
729	- `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean across both
730	  members.
731	- `cargo test` — 22 pass (5 in actor-x1 runtime tests + 17 in
732	  tprobe's moved unit tests) = 22/22. Same count as before the
733	  move, confirming no test was lost.
734	- `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1` replaces the prior
735	  `actor-x1 v0.2.0-1` install; `goal1 --version` and
736	  `goal2 --version` both report `actor-x1 0.2.0-2`.
737	
738	## Rename TProbe2 → TProbe (0.2.0-3)
739	
740	Mechanical rename sweep. `TProbe2` → `TProbe`, `TProbe2RecId`
741	→ `TProbeRecId`, `tprobe2.rs` → `tprobe.rs`, and the band-table
742	header string `"tprobe2"` → `"tprobe"`. The "2" only carried
743	meaning inside upstream `iiac-perf`, where it distinguished
744	this scope-API probe from a sibling direct-histogram `TProbe`.
745	Inside this standalone crate there's only one probe, so the
746	suffix is noise.
747	
748	- `Cargo.lock`: updated by cargo for the tprobe version bump.
749	- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.2.0-2` → `0.2.0-3`.
750	- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs`: six textual occurrences of
751	  `TProbe2` → `TProbe` (field type, `use`, constructor call,
752	  `probe_mut` return type, and two doc-links).
753	- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`: docstring phrase
754	  `` `tprobe2` band-table report `` → `` `tprobe` band-table
755	  report ``.
756	- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: two copies of the same
757	  docstring phrase updated.
758	- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.0` → `0.1.1` (patch bump —
759	  public API rename).
760	- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: module declaration
761	  `pub mod tprobe2` → `pub mod tprobe`; re-export
762	  `pub use tprobe2::{TProbe2, TProbe2RecId}` → `pub use
763	  tprobe::{TProbe, TProbeRecId}`; two docstring references
764	  (`[`tprobe2`]` → `[`tprobe`]`, `[`TProbe2`]` → `[`TProbe`]`).
765	- `crates/tprobe/src/tprobe.rs` (was `tprobe2.rs`): file
766	  renamed; all `TProbe2` / `TProbe2RecId` identifier
767	  occurrences → `TProbe` / `TProbeRecId` (struct defs, impls,
768	  tests, doc-links); band-table header string `"tprobe2"` →
769	  `"tprobe"`.
770	- `crates/tprobe/src/overhead.rs`: four doc-link references
771	  `TProbe2` / `TProbe2::report` → `TProbe` / `TProbe::report`.
772	- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`: module-level docstring
773	  reworded — previously referenced both `TProbe` (fast path)
774	  and `TProbe2` (scope API) from upstream; now notes that
775	  only the scope-API probe was vendored and still shares the
776	  renderer. Function docstring example list trimmed from
777	  `(`"tprobe"`, `"tprobe2"`, …)` to `(e.g. `"tprobe"`)`.
778	- `notes/chores-01.md`: this section. Earlier sections that
779	  record the arrival of `TProbe2` during Stage 1 (`0.1.0-2`,
780	  `0.1.0-3`, `0.1.0-6`) are left as-is — they describe events
781	  at the time, when the identifier was literally `TProbe2`.
782	- `notes/todo.md`: `Rename TProbe2 → TProbe (0.2.0-3) [[6]]`
783	  added to `## Done` + reference.
784	
785	### Design decisions recorded here
786	
787	- **Single `replace_all` on `TProbe2` handles both identifiers**
788	  because `TProbe2RecId` contains `TProbe2` as a prefix; the
789	  substring replacement produces `TProbeRecId` without a
790	  separate pass.
791	- **The submodule keeps the name `tprobe`**, matching the crate
792	  name. External callers reach types via the `pub use` at the
793	  crate root (`tprobe::TProbe`); the fully-qualified internal
794	  path `tprobe::tprobe::TProbe` is slightly awkward but never
795	  written by consumers. The alternative — moving the struct
796	  directly into `lib.rs` and eliminating the submodule — would
797	  inflate `lib.rs` with ~290 lines of probe implementation
798	  and make the crate root less navigable. Keeping the
799	  submodule is cheaper for readability.
800	- **tprobe version bumps `0.1.0` → `0.1.1`** rather than
801	  `0.2.0`. Pre-1.0 semver calls for a minor bump on API break,
802	  but `actor-x1` is the only consumer, is updated atomically
803	  in the same commit, and doesn't pin a version requirement
804	  (path deps skip that). The bump is informational; a patch
805	  level is enough to signal "content changed" in cargo's
806	  accounting.
807

852	### What ships in 0.2.0
853	
854	- Two workspace members under `crates/`:
855	  `actor-x1` (the binaries and runtimes) and `tprobe`
856	  (the vendored-from-iiac-perf probe stack).
857	- `tprobe` at version `0.1.1` — standalone crate providing
858	  `TProbe`, `TProbeRecId`, `Overhead` / `calibrate`, and the
859	  `band_table`, `fmt`, `pin`, `ticks` submodules.
860	- `actor-x1` picks up `tprobe` via a path dependency; perf
861	  crates (`core_affinity`, `hdrhistogram`, `minstant`) no
862	  longer declared directly.
863	- Session-flow conventions codified in CLAUDE.md:
864	  `vc-x1 push` invocation, prerequisite `.gitignore` entry

```json
  "new_string": "### What's next\n\nStage 2 (see [design.md](design.md)): three actors with a\nricher `Message { src_id, dst_id, send_count }`, per-actor\nconstructors, and names. `tprobe`'s API settled enough in\nthis ladder that promoting it to a sibling repo (if ever\nwanted) would now be a straightforward copy rather than\nanother extraction round.\n\n## Band-table format + overhead docs — plan marker (0.3.0-0)\n\nReworks the band-table report so the existing columns show\nraw (unadjusted) values and a new rightmost `adj mean` column\nshows the overhead-subtracted mean. Along the way, formalizes\nwhat \"overhead\" means in `tprobe` — framing, loop-per-iter,\nand an unmeasurable \"everything else\" category — and extends\nthe subtraction to include `loop_per_iter_ticks` on top of\nframing. CLI is simplified: `--raw` goes away (calibration is\n~10 ms, always run), and the `--warmup` default drops from 10 s\nto 0.5 s so quick iteration doesn't require typing\n`--warmup 0` every time. Notes are split per crate in\npreparation for eventually separating the two crates into\ntheir own repos.\n\nMulti-step ladder:\n\n- `0.3.0-0` — Plan marker. Bump `actor-x1` to `0.3.0-0`; this\n  chore section; `notes/todo.md` entry under `## In Progress`.\n  No structural change.\n- `0.3.0-1` — Notes reorg + per-crate `README.md` +\n  `overhead-model.md`. Move `notes/design.md` →\n  `crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md`. Create\n  `crates/actor-x1/notes/`, `crates/tprobe/notes/`,\n  `crates/actor-x1/README.md`, `crates/tprobe/README.md`.\n  Write `crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md` defining\n  framing, loop-per-iter, and the unmeasurable-overhead\n  category (cache misses, branch mispredicts, context\n  switches, interrupts — they stay in the measurement because\n  we can't attribute them per-event). Replace long prose in\n  `overhead.rs` / `tprobe.rs` doc comments with pointers to\n  `overhead-model.md`. Docs-only; no behavior change.\n- `0.3.0-2` — Extend subtraction to framing + loop-per-iter.\n  `Overhead::per_event_ticks(batch)` returns\n  `framing_ticks / batch + loop_per_iter_ticks` instead of\n  just `framing_ticks / batch`. `apparatus:` line shows both\n  components so readers see the split. Tests updated.\n- `0.3.0-3` — Band-table `adj mean` column. `TProbe::report`\n  stores raw per-event values in the histogram (no correction\n  applied). `band_table::render` gains an optional per-event\n  correction; existing `first` / `last` / `range` / `count` /\n  `mean` columns show raw; new rightmost `adj mean` column\n  shows `raw − correction`. Summary rows `mean` and\n  `mean min-p99` get adj values in the new column; `stdev`\n  rows leave it blank (shift-invariant). Uses global-average\n  correction (exact for today's constant-batch PoC; a note in\n  `overhead-model.md` flags where per-band tracking would be\n  needed if batches ever mix within one probe).\n- `0.3.0-4` — Remove `--raw` flag. Drop the flag from `goal1`\n  and `goal2`; calibration runs unconditionally. Drop the\n  \"raw (no overhead subtraction)\" apparatus-line branch.\n- `0.3.0-5` — Warmup default `10.0 → 0.5`. Both binaries.\n- `0.3.0` — Closing marker. Drop `-N` suffix on `actor-x1`;\n  top-level `README.md` updated for new output format, no\n  `--raw`, new warmup default, per-crate READMEs. Move\n  `notes/todo.md` entries to `## Done`.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **Extend subtraction to `loop_per_iter_ticks`.** Earlier\n  (`0.1.0-6`) the subtraction was deliberately scoped to\n  framing only, on the grounds that \"the real dispatch loop's\n  per-iter cost is part of what the user wants to measure\".\n  Revisited: the real loop's scaffolding (loop branch,\n  counter increment) is structural overhead relative to the\n  dispatch work, and `black_box(1)`'s `loop_per_iter_ticks`\n  is a close-enough proxy for it. Biased slightly low\n  because the real loop doesn't execute the `black_box`\n  itself; the bias is small and worth the simpler story in\n  the output. `overhead-model.md` records this tradeoff\n  explicitly.\n- **Global average correction, not per-band.** Correction\n  depends on `batch`; in the current PoC every record in a\n  given probe has the same `batch`, so a single constant\n  correction applies uniformly. If later probes ever mix\n  `batch` sizes within one drain, a per-band correction sum\n  would be more faithful. Deferred until that situation\n  actually arises; `overhead-model.md` flags the extension\n  point.\n- **Per-crate `notes/` and `README.md`.** Anticipates\n  splitting the crates into separate repos later. Workspace\n  `/notes/` keeps format conventions, VCS tips, release\n  chronology, workspace-level todo/done; each crate's own\n  design, architecture, and model notes travel with it."
}
```

> AGENT

Added. Thoughts on the idea:

**Strong fit for a benchmark harness.** Both `linkme` (distributed slice via linker) and `inventory` (ctor-based) let a file drop in a `bench!` macro invocation and have the runner pick it up with zero `main` edits. That's the same ergonomic as criterion-rs. With `tprobe` providing the measurement primitive, a small runner loops over the registered entries, sets up warmup/calibration/pin config once, and reports each benchmark's band table. Very clean.

**Partial fit for actors.** Registration works for *discovery* — "enumerate every `Actor` type the binary knows about" is exactly what distributed slices give you — but actor construction still needs runtime config (id, name, channel endpoints, thread placement). So linkme/inventory replaces the "hand-written registry lookup table" but doesn't replace the wiring in `main` / runtime setup. Worth using if we find ourselves editing a registry table every time we add an actor; not a silver bullet for the whole init problem.

**Tradeoffs to validate before committing hard:**

- `linkme` relies on linker section magic. Works on x86_64 Linux out of the box but can break under aggressive strip/LTO or unusual link modes. Worth a smoke test across `--release` + whatever flags we normally run.
- `inventory` uses static constructors (via `ctor`). Broader platform support, pays a small init-time cost, and has had historical issues when the whole-binary reachability analysis elides unused statics — a registration in a test-only file can silently not register. Usually fine in a binary crate, just something to verify.
- Both lose the "you can see every registered item by grepping `main.rs`" property, which some people dislike on maintainability grounds. Matter of taste.

**My take**: do it for the benchmark harness first (clear win), evaluate from there whether the actor side is worth it (probably a smaller win).

Nothing for me to do with those thoughts beyond the todo entry — I didn't commit to the approach, just flagged it for later.

Work complete for **0.3.0-0**. Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

```json
  "new_string": "- **Per-crate `notes/` and `README.md`.** Anticipates\n  splitting the crates into separate repos later. Workspace\n  `/notes/` keeps format conventions, VCS tips, release\n  chronology, workspace-level todo/done; each crate's own\n  design, architecture, and model notes travel with it.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness (0.3.0-0)\n\nIdea captured during `0.3.0-0`; not scheduled for\nimplementation yet. Explores using compile-time static\nregistration (`linkme` distributed slices or `inventory` via\n`ctor`) to build a `tprobe`-driven benchmark runner where\nindividual benchmarks declare themselves in separate files\nand the runner picks them up with no `main()` edits per\nbenchmark — the ergonomic pattern criterion-rs offers.\n\n### Fit for benchmarks\n\nThe bot thinks this is a strong fit. Each benchmark file\ndrops in a `bench!(...)` invocation; the runner iterates\nthe registered entries, sets up warmup/calibration/pin\nconfig once, and prints a `tprobe` band table per\nbenchmark. The measurement primitive already exists\n(`tprobe`); the missing piece is the registration and\niteration layer. Result: adding a benchmark is a\none-file change with no central table to edit.\n\n### Fit for actor applications\n\nThe bot thinks this is a partial fit. Registration gives\ndiscovery (\"enumerate every `Actor` type the binary\nknows\"), but actor construction still needs runtime config\n— id, name, channel endpoints, thread placement — which\nlives in `main` / runtime setup rather than at declaration\nsites. Useful if we find ourselves hand-editing a registry\ntable per new actor; not a silver bullet for the full\nwiring problem.\n\n### Tradeoffs to validate before committing\n\n- `linkme` uses linker-section magic. Works on\n  `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` (our target) out of the box;\n  the bot thinks aggressive `strip` / LTO / unusual link\n  modes could in principle drop registrations, so a\n  release-mode smoke test with our actual flags is worth\n  doing before relying on it.\n- `inventory` uses static constructors (via `ctor`).\n  Broader platform support, pays a small init-time cost.\n  The bot thinks the main historical failure mode is\n  reachability elision — a registration in a\n  conditionally-compiled or test-only module silently not\n  registering — which is usually fine in a binary crate\n  but worth a sanity check.\n- Both approaches lose the \"grep `main.rs` to see every\n  registered item\" property. Taste call; the bot thinks\n  the file-per-benchmark ergonomic outweighs it for\n  benchmarks, and the call is less clear for actors.\n\n### Recommendation\n\nThe bot's suggested order: start with the benchmark\nharness (clear win, bounded scope), learn the mechanism,\nthen decide whether the actor-registration case justifies\na second use. Tracked as a todo entry (`[9]`) against this\nsection; promote to a versioned plan marker when scheduled."
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/tprobe/README.md",
  "content": "# tprobe\n\nA named, single-writer performance probe that records\nhardware tick-counter deltas across scoped code regions and\nrenders a percentile band-table report.\n\nPart of the [actor-x1 workspace](../..). Originally vendored\nfrom [`iiac-perf`](https://github.com/winksaville/iiac-perf);\nextracted into its own crate at `actor-x1 0.2.0`.\n\n## At a glance\n\n```rust\nuse tprobe::{TProbe, calibrate};\n\nlet mut probe = TProbe::new(\"dispatch\");\nlet ovh = calibrate();\n\nfor _ in 0..n {\n    let id = probe.start(site_id);\n    // ... work to measure ...\n    probe.end(id);\n}\n\nprobe.report(/* as_ticks = */ false, Some(&ovh));\n```\n\n## Public surface\n\n- `TProbe` / `TProbeRecId` — scope-based probe\n  (`start` / `end` / `end_batch` → record buffer →\n  histogram on `report`).\n- `Overhead` / `calibrate` — apparatus-overhead calibration\n  via a two-point fit.\n- Modules `pin` (CPU affinity) and `ticks` (hardware tick\n  counter).\n\n## Notes\n\n- [`notes/design.md`](notes/design.md) — architecture and\n  hot-path / cold-path split.\n- [`notes/overhead-model.md`](notes/overhead-model.md) —\n  formal overhead model: what framing is, what\n  `loop_per_iter` is, which overhead is unmeasurable and\n  stays in the measurement, and the current subtraction\n  policy.\n\n## Platform\n\nx86_64 only; `ticks.rs` emits `compile_error!` on other\narchitectures.\n\n## License\n\nDual-licensed under MIT OR Apache-2.0.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/tprobe/notes/design.md",
  "content": "# tprobe design\n\nA named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter\ndeltas, plus a record buffer, plus a percentile band-table\nrenderer. Tuned for the hot path to be as short as possible\n— two `rdtsc` reads and a `Vec::push` — with delta math,\nhistogram ingestion, and tick→ns conversion deferred to\nreport time.\n\n## Data flow\n\n```\nstart(site_id)  ─┐\n                 │\nend(id)          ├─► Record { site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc, batch }\n                 │             └─ appended to records: Vec<Record>\nend_batch(id, n) ┘\n\n                            report(as_ticks, overhead)\n                                   │\n                                   ▼\n                        records.drain(..) →\n                          per_event = (delta - correction) / batch\n                                   │\n                                   ▼\n                             Histogram<u64>\n                                   │\n                                   ▼\n                          band_table::render\n```\n\n- **Hot path** is `start` + `end*`: two `rdtsc` reads and a\n  `Vec::push`. No division, no histogram updates, no unit\n  conversion.\n- **Cold path** is `report`: drains the record buffer,\n  applies per-record division and overhead correction,\n  ingests into the histogram, then renders the band table.\n- **`clear`** zeroes both the record buffer and the\n  histogram. Used at the warmup → measurement boundary so\n  warmup samples don't contaminate the reported\n  distribution.\n\n## Modules\n\n- `tprobe` — the probe type (`TProbe`, `TProbeRecId`) and\n  the `start` / `end` / `end_batch` / `clear` / `report`\n  surface.\n- `overhead` — calibration and the `Overhead` struct. See\n  [`overhead-model.md`](overhead-model.md) for the formal\n  model.\n- `band_table` — percentile-band renderer (12 bands,\n  `first` / `last` / `range` / `count` / `mean` columns,\n  summary rows). Shared by the `TProbe` report path.\n- `ticks` — hardware tick-counter abstraction. x86_64 only;\n  uses `rdtsc` via the `minstant` crate. Other architectures\n  emit `compile_error!`.\n- `fmt` — `fmt_commas` / `fmt_commas_f64`, extracted from\n  `iiac-perf`'s harness so we don't carry the whole 393-line\n  benchmark harness for two formatting helpers.\n- `pin` — CPU-affinity helpers (`parse_cores`,\n  `pin_current`). Adapted from `iiac-perf`'s `pin.rs`.\n\n## Upstream\n\nVendored from\n[`iiac-perf`](https://github.com/winksaville/iiac-perf) —\nsource path at the time of vendoring: `../iiac-perf/src`.\nGraduated from `actor-x1`'s in-tree `src/perf/` module at\n`actor-x1 0.2.0-2`.\n\nDivergences from upstream at the time of graduation:\n\n- Import paths reflect the standalone-crate layout\n  (`crate::x` rather than `crate::perf::x`).\n- `// OK: …` justifications on every `unwrap*` call per the\n  host workspace's code conventions.\n- `ticks.rs` diagnostic string mentions \"tprobe\" rather than\n  \"iiac-perf\".\n- `TProbe` here corresponds to upstream's scope-API probe\n  (`TProbe2` in `iiac-perf`); the upstream fast-path\n  direct-histogram `TProbe` was not vendored. The \"2\" suffix\n  was dropped on extraction at `0.2.0-3`.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md",
  "content": "# Overhead model\n\nDefinitions and policy for what `tprobe` calls \"overhead\"\nand what gets subtracted from measured values.\n\n## Measured quantities\n\nCalibration runs a two-point fit on an empty `black_box(1)`\nbench and produces two numbers, both recorded on the\n`Overhead` struct.\n\n### Framing — `framing_ticks` (per scope)\n\nThe hardware cost of the two `rdtsc` reads that bracket\nevery `TProbe` scope. Paid once per scope, independent of\nhow many events the scope covers. On modern x86 this is\n~20 ticks (~5 ns at ~3.7 GHz).\n\nWhy two-point (and not single-point)? `rdtsc` is not a\nserializing instruction, so two back-to-back reads with\nnothing between them can execute out-of-order and measure\nas little as 0–1 ticks — well below the true apparatus\ncost. Bracketing with `_mm_lfence` drains the pipeline\nharder than real work does and overcorrects. The two-point\nfit avoids both biases by varying an `inner` size and\nsolving\n\n```\nraw_delta = framing + inner · loop_per_iter\n```\n\nin `f64` arithmetic (no integer truncation) across two\nempty benches at `N_LOW = 100` and `N_HIGH = 10_000`. The\nintercept is framing; the slope is `loop_per_iter` (below).\n\nNoise amplification on the intercept is\n`d(framing) / d(raw_low) = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH − N_LOW) ≈ 1.01`\nwith the current constants — any slop on `raw_low`\npropagates roughly 1:1 to framing.\n\n### Loop-per-iter — `loop_per_iter_ticks` (per inner iter)\n\nThe slope of the same fit — the per-iteration cost of the\ncalibration's empty inner loop, one `black_box(1)` per\niteration. On modern x86 this is small, on the order of one\ntick per iter.\n\nIt models the loop scaffolding a real measurement loop also\npays: loop branch, counter increment, plus whatever cost a\n\"does-nothing\" iteration has. It is not a perfect model of\na real dispatch loop — the real loop doesn't execute the\n`black_box(1)` itself, so its per-iter scaffolding is\nslightly cheaper than what calibration reports.\n\n## Unmeasurable overhead\n\nThings calibration does not separately measure and that are\nnot separately subtractable from stored scope deltas:\n\n- Cache misses and branch mispredicts inside the scope.\n- Context switches and OS interrupts during the scope.\n- Microarchitectural noise (TLB misses, frequency changes,\n  thermal throttling).\n\nThese are part of what the real system experiences and\nremain in the measurement. The bot thinks they typically\nmanifest in the `p99-max` tail of the histogram — which is\nwhy the band-table report also prints `mean min-p99`\n(whole-distribution statistics excluding the top 1 % tail,\nrobust against rare outliers).\n\nNo future calibration can separate these per-event without\na fundamentally different measurement technique; they are\nthe irreducible noise floor of \"whatever happened between\nthe two `rdtsc` reads\". Keeping them in the measurement is\nhonest — they are part of the real cost the measured code\npays.\n\n## Subtraction policy\n\n`Overhead::per_event_ticks(batch)` returns the per-event\ncorrection `TProbe::report` subtracts from every stored\nvalue. Currently:\n\n```\ncorrection = framing_ticks / batch\n                └── amortized over the scope\n```\n\nAt `batch = 1` the full framing cost is paid per event; at\n`batch = N` it is spread across `N` events, rounding toward\nzero as `N` grows. `loop_per_iter_ticks` is kept on the\n`Overhead` struct for diagnostic visibility but is not\ncurrently subtracted.\n\nThe bot thinks extending subtraction to include\n`loop_per_iter_ticks` is the right call: loop scaffolding\nis overhead relative to the dispatch work, and the\n`black_box(1)` iter cost is a close-enough proxy. The\ntradeoff is a small downward bias on reported values\nbecause the real loop doesn't execute the `black_box(1)`\nitself; the bias is on the order of a tick per event and\nworth the simpler story in the output. That change is\nplanned for a later step in the 0.3.0 ladder; this document\ngets revised then.\n\n## Per-event correction when batches mix\n\nToday every record in a given probe drain carries the same\n`batch` (the runtime commits to one `inner` per run), so\n`correction` is a single constant across the drain. If a\nfuture probe ever mixes `batch` values within one drain —\ne.g. an actor whose scopes cover both fast-path singles\n(`batch = 1`, framing term paid in full) and slow-path\nbatches (`batch = 1000`, framing term ≈ 0) — the correct\noverhead-adjusted mean per band requires per-record\ncorrection tracked per band, not a single global average.\nThe bot thinks the change at that point is:\n\n- drain loop accumulates `band_correction_sum[i]` alongside\n  `band_sum[i]`,\n- `adj_mean[i] = (band_sum[i] - band_correction_sum[i]) /\n  band_count[i]`.\n\nDeferred until that situation arises.\n"
}
```

```
1	//! actor-x1 — an experiment in the actor model in Rust.
2	//!
3	//! See `notes/design.md` for the staged design. Stage 1 provides
4	//! a minimal runtime with two actors that ping-pong an empty
5	//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration; Goal1 runs both
6	//! actors on one thread, Goal2 runs each actor on its own thread.
7	//!
8	//! Public surface today:
9	//! - [`Message`]: the empty unit message Stage 1 actors exchange.
10	//! - [`Actor`]: the single-method actor trait.
11	//! - [`Context`]: the send-side handle passed to each dispatch.
12	//! - [`runtime`]: concrete runtimes (single-thread for Goal1; the
13	//!   multi-thread runtime will land in 0.1.0-3).
14	
15	pub mod runtime;
16	
17	/// Empty unit-type message exchanged between Stage 1 actors.
18	/// Carries no payload by design — Stage 1's contract is only
19	/// "dispatch happens"; Stage 2 replaces this with a richer type.
20	pub struct Message;
21	
22	/// Actor behavior: handle one [`Message`] at a time, never blocking.
23	///
24	/// The only method is [`Actor::handle_message`]; all outbound
25	/// communication goes through the [`Context`] the runtime supplies.
26	pub trait Actor {
27	    /// Handle a single incoming message. Use `ctx` to emit any
28	    /// resulting sends. Must not block.
29	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, msg: Message);
30	}
31	
32	/// Runtime-supplied send handle passed to each
33	/// [`Actor::handle_message`] call. Implementations differ between
34	/// runtimes: the single-thread runtime writes to a shared queue;
35	/// the (future) multi-thread runtime writes to per-actor channels.
36	pub trait Context {
37	    /// Enqueue `msg` for delivery to the actor identified by
38	    /// `dst_id`. Returns immediately; delivery is the runtime's job.
39	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message);
40	}
41	
```

```json
  "old_string": "//! Apparatus-overhead calibration for [`TProbe`]. Measures the\n//! hardware floor cost of two back-to-back `rdtsc` reads — the\n//! bias contaminating every probe scope's stored `end_tsc -\n//! start_tsc` delta — via a two-point fit matching the approach\n//! in `iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs`.\n//!\n//! ### Why two-point, not single-point\n//!\n//! `rdtsc` isn't a serializing instruction. Two back-to-back\n//! reads with nothing between can execute out-of-order and\n//! measure a delta of 0–1 ticks — far below the true framing\n//! cost. Bracketing with `_mm_lfence` overcorrects (lfence drains\n//! the pipeline, which real work doesn't fully do), so single-\n//! point measurement is biased in both directions.\n//!\n//! Two-point fits a line through empty benches at two `inner`\n//! sizes (`N_LOW`, `N_HIGH`):\n//! - raw delta at inner=N = framing + N · loop_per_iter\n//! - slope between (N_LOW, raw_low) and (N_HIGH, raw_high) gives\n//!   loop_per_iter; intercept gives framing.\n//!\n//! The `black_box(1)` inside the inner loop keeps OoO from\n//! collapsing iterations and gives `loop_per_iter` a consistent\n//! value. Noise amplification on framing:\n//! `d(framing)/d(raw_low) = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH − N_LOW)`. At\n//! 100/10_000 that's ~1.01 — any slop on `raw_low` propagates\n//! roughly 1:1 to framing.\n//!\n//! We fit using raw tick deltas (not per-event values) in `f64`\n//! arithmetic so integer truncation can't hide small framing\n//! components.\n//!\n//! ### What gets subtracted\n//!\n//! Only framing. The two-point fit also yields\n//! `loop_per_iter_ticks` (cost of one black_box iteration), which\n//! we keep on the [`Overhead`] struct for diagnostics but do not\n//! subtract from probe samples — the caller's inner-loop cost\n//! (queue pop, deadline check, etc.) is part of what the user is\n//! actually measuring, and the `black_box(1)` loop_per_iter\n//! doesn't model it anyway.\n//!\n//! [`TProbe`]: crate::TProbe\n//! [`TProbe::report`]: crate::TProbe::report",
  "new_string": "//! Apparatus-overhead calibration for [`TProbe`]. Runs a\n//! two-point fit on an empty `black_box(1)` bench and produces\n//! an [`Overhead`] struct; [`TProbe::report`] subtracts the\n//! per-event correction from each stored scope delta.\n//!\n//! For the formal model — framing, `loop_per_iter`, the\n//! unmeasurable \"everything else\" category, the two-point\n//! derivation, and the current subtraction policy — see\n//! `notes/overhead-model.md`.\n//!\n//! [`TProbe`]: crate::TProbe\n//! [`TProbe::report`]: crate::TProbe::report"
}
```

Proposed commit:

**Title**
```
docs: per-crate notes + overhead-model.md (0.3.0-1)
```

**Body**
```
Docs-only step. Each crate gains its own `notes/` directory
and `README.md` so they travel with the crate if the
workspace ever splits into separate repos. Formalizes the
`tprobe` overhead vocabulary in a dedicated
`overhead-model.md` and shortens the long prose blocks in
`overhead.rs` / `lib.rs` to brief summaries plus pointers.

- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.0-0 → 0.3.0-1`.
- `crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md`: moved from
  `/notes/design.md` (unchanged content).
- `crates/actor-x1/README.md`: new per-crate README.
- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `design.md` path updated.
- `crates/tprobe/README.md`: new per-crate README.
- `crates/tprobe/notes/design.md`: new — architecture,
  hot/cold-path split, module layout, upstream history.
- `crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`: new — formal
  definitions of framing and `loop_per_iter`, the
  unmeasurable category (cache misses, context switches,
  interrupts, …), current subtraction policy, and the
  per-band-correction extension if batches ever mix.
- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: 28-line header → 6 lines +
  notes pointers.
- `crates/tprobe/src/overhead.rs`: 42-line header → 10 lines
  + `notes/overhead-model.md` pointer.
- `README.md`, `notes/README.md`, `notes/chores-01.md`:
  `design.md` link targets retargeted to the new location
  (text preserved).
- `notes/chores-01.md`: new chore section for this step;
  linkme/inventory future-work section had its version
  suffix dropped since it isn't scheduled yet.
- `notes/todo.md`: `0.3.0-0` plan-marker → Done; `0.3.0-1`
  entry under In Progress; reference `[10]` added.
```

OK to run `vc-x1 push main`?

> DEVELOPER

Should they get license files, will make it easier to move in the future, but also noise for now

> AGENT

Yes, I'd add them. The move-to-separate-repo property is the whole reason we did per-crate notes/READMEs; license files serve the same goal. Concrete benefits:

- `cargo package`/`cargo publish` only bundles files in the crate directory (not workspace-root ones) unless you rig `include =` in Cargo.toml. Per-crate license files mean the published crate on crates.io has them where users expect.
- Splitting into a separate repo becomes a clean `mv crates/tprobe ../tprobe-repo/` with nothing to chase down.

Cost is four static files that nobody reads and that basically never change. Small one-time noise, permanent benefit.

If approved I'll:

1. Copy `LICENSE-MIT` and `LICENSE-APACHE` from the workspace root to `crates/actor-x1/` and `crates/tprobe/`.
2. Update the READMEs so they stop pointing at the workspace-root license files (link to local ones instead).
3. Amend the 0.3.0-1 chore section + commit body.
4. Re-run the pre-commit checklist (fmt/clippy/test — unaffected by text files, but worth verifying).

Want me to roll this into 0.3.0-1 before we push?

```
1	//! Goal1 binary: two actors on a single thread ping-pong an empty
2	//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration in seconds (f64),
3	//! after an optional warmup phase. Prints messages-handled /
4	//! throughput and a `tprobe` band-table report of per-dispatch
5	//! latency.
6	//!
7	//! Usage: `goal1 --help`
8	
9	use std::time::Duration;
10	
11	use actor_x1::runtime::SingleThreadRuntime;
12	use actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};
13	use clap::Parser;
14	use tprobe::{self as perf, pin, ticks};
15	
16	/// Actor that, on every message received, sends exactly one message
17	/// back to its configured peer.
18	struct PingPongActor {
19	    peer_id: u32,
20	}
21	
22	impl Actor for PingPongActor {
23	    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.
24	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
25	        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);
26	    }
27	}
28	
29	/// CLI for goal1: single-thread two-actor ping-pong with warmup,
30	/// per-dispatch probe batching, and ns-or-ticks band-table report.
31	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
32	#[command(
33	    version,
34	    about,
35	    long_about = None,
36	    max_term_width = 80,
37	    before_help = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")),
38	)]
39	struct Cli {
40	    /// Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup).
41	    #[arg(value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
42	    duration_s: f64,
43	
44	    /// Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the
45	    /// same dispatch loop as the measurement phase (probe active)
46	    /// so cache/branch-predictor/frequency land in the same state
47	    /// by the time the probe is cleared and measurement begins.
48	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0, value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
49	    warmup: f64,
50	
51	    /// Number of dispatches per probe scope. Larger values
52	    /// amortize probe apparatus overhead and push stored tick
53	    /// values into a range where the histogram's 0.1 %-relative
54	    /// buckets resolve small variations.
55	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 1, value_parser = clap::value_parser!(u64).range(1..))]
56	    inner: u64,
57	
58	    /// Display probe values as raw ticks instead of nanoseconds.
59	    #[arg(short = 't', long)]
60	    ticks: bool,
61	
62	    /// Skip apparatus-overhead calibration and report uncorrected
63	    /// per-event values. Use to see the raw cost the probe sees,
64	    /// including its own two-rdtsc framing (~4 ns on modern x86).
65	    #[arg(long)]
66	    raw: bool,
67	
68	    /// Pin the workload thread to a logical CPU. Accepts a single
69	    /// id or a comma-separated / range list; only the first core
70	    /// is used (goal1 is single-threaded). Tightens stdev by
71	    /// eliminating OS thread migration noise.
72	    #[arg(long)]
73	    pin: Option<String>,
74	}
75	
76	/// `value_parser` helper: reject negative, NaN, and infinite values.
77	fn parse_non_negative_secs(s: &str) -> Result<f64, String> {
78	    let v: f64 = s
79	        .parse()
80	        .map_err(|e: std::num::ParseFloatError| e.to_string())?;
81	    if !v.is_finite() || v < 0.0 {
82	        Err(format!("'{s}' is not a non-negative finite number"))
83	    } else {
84	        Ok(v)
85	    }
86	}
87	
88	/// Parse CLI, run warmup + measurement on two single-thread
89	/// ping-pong actors, print message count + throughput, then
90	/// render the per-dispatch band-table report.
91	fn main() {
92	    let cli = Cli::parse();
93	
94	    println!("{} {}", env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),);
95	
96	    // Pin the main thread before any work — calibration and the
97	    // dispatch loop both run here, so they share the pinned core.
98	    let pin_core: Option<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
99	        None => None,
100	        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
101	            Ok(cores) => cores.first().copied(),
102	            Err(e) => {
103	                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
104	                std::process::exit(2);
105	            }
106	        },
107	    };
108	    pin::pin_current(pin_core);
109	
110	    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("goal1.dispatch");
111	    let a_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));
112	    let b_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));
113	    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));
114	    rt.seed(a_id, Message);
115	
116	    // Warmup: same loop, probe active, records discarded at the boundary.
117	    rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup), cli.inner);
118	    rt.probe_mut().clear();
119	
120	    // Calibration (skipped with --raw). Runs on the warmed system,
121	    // before measurement begins, so freq/cache state matches what
122	    // the probe will see during measurement.
123	    let overhead = if cli.raw {
124	        None
125	    } else {
126	        Some(perf::calibrate())
127	    };
128	
129	    // Measurement.
130	    let count = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s), cli.inner);
131	    let mmps = count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;
132	    println!(
133	        "goal1: {count} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, inner={})",
134	        cli.duration_s, cli.inner,
135	    );
136	    match pin_core {
137	        Some(c) => println!("  pinning: main → core {c}"),
138	        None => println!("  pinning: none (unpinned)"),
139	    }
140	    if let Some(ovh) = &overhead {
141	        let framing_ns = ovh.framing_ticks as f64 / ticks::ticks_per_ns();
142	        let per_event_tk = ovh.per_event_ticks(cli.inner);
143	        let per_event_ns = per_event_tk as f64 / ticks::ticks_per_ns();
144	        println!(
145	            "  apparatus: framing={} tk ({:.2} ns); per-event at inner={} = {} tk ({:.2} ns)",
146	            ovh.framing_ticks, framing_ns, cli.inner, per_event_tk, per_event_ns,
147	        );
148	    } else {
149	        println!("  apparatus: raw (no overhead subtraction)");
150	    }
151	    println!();
152	    rt.probe_mut().report(cli.ticks, overhead.as_ref());
153	}
154	
```

```
1	//! Goal2 binary: two actors, each on its own thread, ping-ponging
2	//! an empty [`Message`] over `std::sync::mpsc` channels for a
3	//! caller-supplied duration in seconds, after an optional warmup.
4	//! Prints messages-handled / throughput, an apparatus-overhead
5	//! diagnostic, and a `tprobe` band-table report per actor.
6	//!
7	//! Unlike goal1, `inner` is fixed at 1: ping-pong keeps at most
8	//! one message in flight per channel, so batched probe scopes
9	//! could never fill. If we later add a workload with deeper
10	//! per-channel queues we'll revisit.
11	//!
12	//! Usage: `goal2 --help`
13	
14	use std::time::Duration;
15	
16	use actor_x1::runtime::MultiThreadRuntime;
17	use actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};
18	use clap::Parser;
19	use tprobe::{self as perf, pin, ticks};
20	
21	/// Actor that, on every message received, sends exactly one
22	/// message back to its configured peer. Same as goal1's but must
23	/// also be `Send` to cross thread boundaries.
24	struct PingPongActor {
25	    peer_id: u32,
26	}
27	
28	impl Actor for PingPongActor {
29	    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.
30	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
31	        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);
32	    }
33	}
34	
35	/// CLI for goal2: two-thread two-actor ping-pong with warmup,
36	/// per-dispatch probe, apparatus-overhead subtraction, and
37	/// ns-or-ticks band-table report per actor.
38	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
39	#[command(
40	    version,
41	    about,
42	    long_about = None,
43	    max_term_width = 80,
44	    before_help = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")),
45	)]
46	struct Cli {
47	    /// Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup).
48	    #[arg(value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
49	    duration_s: f64,
50	
51	    /// Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the
52	    /// same dispatch loop as the measurement phase (probe active)
53	    /// so cache / branch-predictor / frequency land in the same
54	    /// state by the time the probe is cleared and measurement
55	    /// begins.
56	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0, value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
57	    warmup: f64,
58	
59	    /// Display probe values as raw ticks instead of nanoseconds.
60	    #[arg(short = 't', long)]
61	    ticks: bool,
62	
63	    /// Skip apparatus-overhead calibration and report uncorrected
64	    /// per-event values.
65	    #[arg(long)]
66	    raw: bool,
67	
68	    /// Pin each actor thread to a logical CPU. Accepts a
69	    /// comma-separated / range list; actor `i` pins to
70	    /// `pin[i % pin.len()]`. Examples: `--pin 0,1` pairs two
71	    /// actors to two CPUs; `--pin 0` oversubscribes both onto
72	    /// one core. Tightens stdev by eliminating OS thread
73	    /// migration noise.
74	    #[arg(long)]
75	    pin: Option<String>,
76	}
77	
78	/// `value_parser` helper: reject negative, NaN, and infinite values.
79	fn parse_non_negative_secs(s: &str) -> Result<f64, String> {
80	    let v: f64 = s
81	        .parse()
82	        .map_err(|e: std::num::ParseFloatError| e.to_string())?;
83	    if !v.is_finite() || v < 0.0 {
84	        Err(format!("'{s}' is not a non-negative finite number"))
85	    } else {
86	        Ok(v)
87	    }
88	}
89	
90	/// Parse CLI, calibrate apparatus (unless --raw), run two
91	/// ping-pong actors on their own threads, print summary and
92	/// per-actor `tprobe` band-table reports.
93	fn main() {
94	    let cli = Cli::parse();
95	    println!("{} {}", env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),);
96	
97	    // Calibrate on main thread before spawning. Matches goal1's
98	    // calibration code path; actor threads share the same CPU
99	    // microarchitecture so a single Overhead is good enough.
100	    let overhead = if cli.raw {
101	        None
102	    } else {
103	        Some(perf::calibrate())
104	    };
105	
106	    let mut rt = MultiThreadRuntime::new("goal2.dispatch");
107	    let a_id = rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 });
108	    let b_id = rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 });
109	    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));
110	    rt.seed(a_id, Message);
111	
112	    let warmup = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup);
113	    let measurement = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s);
114	    let pin_cores: Vec<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
115	        None => vec![],
116	        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
117	            Ok(cores) => cores,
118	            Err(e) => {
119	                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
120	                std::process::exit(2);
121	            }
122	        },
123	    };
124	    let results = rt.run(warmup, measurement, &pin_cores);
125	
126	    let total_count: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();
127	    let mmps = total_count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;
128	    println!(
129	        "goal2: {total_count} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, {n} actors, inner=1)",
130	        cli.duration_s,
131	        n = results.len(),
132	    );
133	    if let Some(ovh) = &overhead {
134	        let framing_ns = ovh.framing_ticks as f64 / ticks::ticks_per_ns();
135	        let per_event_tk = ovh.per_event_ticks(1);
136	        let per_event_ns = per_event_tk as f64 / ticks::ticks_per_ns();
137	        println!(
138	            "  apparatus: framing={} tk ({:.2} ns); per-event at inner=1 = {} tk ({:.2} ns)",
139	            ovh.framing_ticks, framing_ns, per_event_tk, per_event_ns,
140	        );
141	    } else {
142	        println!("  apparatus: raw (no overhead subtraction)");
143	    }
144	    if pin_cores.is_empty() {
145	        println!("  pinning: none (unpinned)");
146	    } else {
147	        let n = results.len();
148	        let plan: Vec<String> = (0..n)
149	            .map(|i| format!("actor{i}→core{}", pin_cores[i % pin_cores.len()]))
150	            .collect();
151	        println!("  pinning: {}", plan.join(", "));
152	    }
153	    println!();
154	
155	    for (i, (count, mut probe)) in results.into_iter().enumerate() {
156	        println!("  actor {i}: handled {count} messages");
157	        probe.report(cli.ticks, overhead.as_ref());
158	    }
159	}
160	
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/tprobe/src/overhead.rs",
  "old_string": "impl Overhead {\n    /// Per-event framing correction at a given `batch`, in ticks:\n    /// the framing is paid once per scope and amortized across\n    /// `batch` events. Integer division truncates toward zero, so\n    /// at `batch > framing_ticks` the correction rounds to zero.\n    pub fn per_event_ticks(&self, batch: u64) -> u64 {\n        self.framing_ticks / batch.max(1)\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "impl Overhead {\n    /// Per-event overhead correction at a given `batch`, in ticks.\n    /// Framing is paid once per scope and amortized across `batch`\n    /// events; `loop_per_iter_ticks` is paid every inner iteration.\n    /// The sum is rounded to the nearest tick.\n    ///\n    /// See `notes/overhead-model.md` for the formal model.\n    pub fn per_event_ticks(&self, batch: u64) -> u64 {\n        let framing_per_event = self.framing_ticks as f64 / batch.max(1) as f64;\n        let correction = framing_per_event + self.loop_per_iter_ticks;\n        correction.round().max(0.0) as u64\n    }\n}"
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn per_event_ticks_amortizes_framing() {\n        let ovh = Overhead {\n            framing_ticks: 20,\n            loop_per_iter_ticks: 0.0,\n            cal_raw_low_ticks: 0,\n            cal_raw_high_ticks: 0,\n            cal_duration: Duration::from_millis(1),\n        };\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(1), 20);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(10), 2);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(100), 0); // truncates\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(0), 20); // batch=0 treated as 1\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn per_event_ticks_amortizes_framing() {\n        let ovh = Overhead {\n            framing_ticks: 20,\n            loop_per_iter_ticks: 0.0,\n            cal_raw_low_ticks: 0,\n            cal_raw_high_ticks: 0,\n            cal_duration: Duration::from_millis(1),\n        };\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(1), 20);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(10), 2);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(100), 0); // 0.2 rounds to 0\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(0), 20); // batch=0 treated as 1\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn per_event_ticks_adds_loop_per_iter() {\n        let ovh = Overhead {\n            framing_ticks: 20,\n            loop_per_iter_ticks: 1.5,\n            cal_raw_low_ticks: 0,\n            cal_raw_high_ticks: 0,\n            cal_duration: Duration::from_millis(1),\n        };\n        // batch=1:   20 + 1.5 = 21.5  → 22\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(1), 22);\n        // batch=10:   2 + 1.5 =  3.5  →  4\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(10), 4);\n        // batch=1000: 0.02 + 1.5 = 1.52 →  2\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(1000), 2);\n    }"
}
```

```json
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md",
  "old_string": "## Subtraction policy\n\n`Overhead::per_event_ticks(batch)` returns the per-event\ncorrection `TProbe::report` subtracts from every stored\nvalue. Currently:\n\n```\ncorrection = framing_ticks / batch\n                └── amortized over the scope\n```\n\nAt `batch = 1` the full framing cost is paid per event; at\n`batch = N` it is spread across `N` events, rounding toward\nzero as `N` grows. `loop_per_iter_ticks` is kept on the\n`Overhead` struct for diagnostic visibility but is not\ncurrently subtracted.\n\nThe bot thinks extending subtraction to include\n`loop_per_iter_ticks` is the right call: loop scaffolding\nis overhead relative to the dispatch work, and the\n`black_box(1)` iter cost is a close-enough proxy. The\ntradeoff is a small downward bias on reported values\nbecause the real loop doesn't execute the `black_box(1)`\nitself; the bias is on the order of a tick per event and\nworth the simpler story in the output. That change is\nplanned for a later step in the 0.3.0 ladder; this document\ngets revised then.",
  "new_string": "## Subtraction policy\n\n`Overhead::per_event_ticks(batch)` returns the per-event\ncorrection `TProbe::report` subtracts from every stored\nvalue:\n\n```\ncorrection = framing_ticks / batch + loop_per_iter_ticks\n                └── amortized       └── paid per inner\n                    over the scope      iteration\n```\n\nAt `batch = 1` the framing term is paid in full; at\n`batch = N` it is spread across `N` events and rounds\ntoward zero as `N` grows. `loop_per_iter_ticks` is added\nback because the real measurement loop pays per-iter\nscaffolding (loop branch + counter increment) that the\nempty-bench fit captures. The sum is rounded to the\nnearest tick.\n\nThe bot thinks this matches \"cost of the work alone\" more\nclosely than framing-only subtraction: the real loop's\nper-iter scaffolding is structural overhead relative to\nthe dispatch work, and `black_box(1)`'s iter cost is a\nreasonable proxy. The tradeoff is a small downward bias —\nthe real loop doesn't execute the `black_box(1)` itself,\nso the measured `loop_per_iter_ticks` is slightly larger\nthan the real loop's scaffolding. The bot thinks the bias\nis on the order of a tick per event and is worth the\nsimpler story in the output."
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "- **License files copied into each crate.** Workspace root\n  still has `LICENSE-MIT` / `LICENSE-APACHE`; each crate\n  now also has its own copies. `cargo package` only bundles\n  files inside the crate directory, so per-crate copies are\n  what ends up in the distributed artifact on crates.io.\n  Splitting a crate into its own repo later becomes a\n  straight directory move with nothing to chase down. Cost\n  is four static files that never change; the bot thinks\n  the payoff outweighs the noise.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness",
  "new_string": "- **License files copied into each crate.** Workspace root\n  still has `LICENSE-MIT` / `LICENSE-APACHE`; each crate\n  now also has its own copies. `cargo package` only bundles\n  files inside the crate directory, so per-crate copies are\n  what ends up in the distributed artifact on crates.io.\n  Splitting a crate into its own repo later becomes a\n  straight directory move with nothing to chase down. Cost\n  is four static files that never change; the bot thinks\n  the payoff outweighs the noise.\n\n## Extend subtraction to framing + loop_per_iter (0.3.0-2)\n\nExtends `Overhead::per_event_ticks` to include the\nper-iteration `loop_per_iter_ticks` cost alongside the\nper-scope amortized framing cost. The real measurement\nloop pays per-iter scaffolding (loop branch + counter\nincrement) that the empty-bench calibration captures as\n`loop_per_iter_ticks`; subtracting it matches \"cost of the\nwork alone\" more closely than framing-only subtraction.\nThe apparatus diagnostic line now shows both components so\nreaders can see the split. Behavior change is display-only\nin the band-table body (raw stored values depend on the\ncorrection); reported numbers drop by ~`loop_per_iter` per\nevent (typically on the order of one tick).\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.0-1` → `0.3.0-2`.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/overhead.rs`:\n  `Overhead::per_event_ticks(batch)` returns\n  `(framing_ticks / batch + loop_per_iter_ticks)` computed\n  in `f64` and rounded to nearest `u64`. Docstring updated\n  to describe both terms and point at `notes/overhead-model.md`.\n  New test `per_event_ticks_adds_loop_per_iter` covers the\n  combined formula at three batch sizes. Existing\n  `per_event_ticks_amortizes_framing` still passes unchanged\n  (it uses `loop_per_iter_ticks = 0.0`); comment tweaked\n  from \"truncates\" to \"rounds\".\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`,\n  `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: `apparatus:` line\n  gains a `loop_per_iter=X.XX tk (X.XX ns)` segment between\n  the framing segment and the per-event total.\n- `crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`: subtraction-policy\n  section rewritten to describe the combined formula. The\n  earlier \"planned for a later step\" language is gone;\n  rationale for including `loop_per_iter` stays (closer\n  match to \"cost of the work alone\", small downward bias\n  because the real loop doesn't run the `black_box(1)`).\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **Round the combined correction, not each term\n  independently.** Framing per event can be sub-tick at\n  large `batch` (e.g. `21 / 1000 = 0.021`), and\n  `loop_per_iter_ticks` can also be fractional. Adding in\n  `f64` and rounding once preserves the combined precision\n  (`0.021 + 1.2 ≈ 1.22 → 1`) rather than independently\n  rounding each term down to zero and subtracting nothing.\n- **`round().max(0.0) as u64`.** Calibration guarantees\n  both terms are non-negative (framing clamps at 0,\n  `loop_per_iter` does the same when the fit goes the\n  wrong way), so the `max(0.0)` is belt-and-suspenders\n  against an out-of-bounds cast. Cheap.\n- **No change to histogram semantics.** `TProbe::report`\n  still subtracts the per-event correction and clamps at\n  1 (the histogram lower bound). Only the correction value\n  changes; the subtraction path is untouched.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"
}
```

```
This file contains near term tasks with a short description
and reference links to more details.

- Extend subtraction to framing + loop_per_iter (0.3.0-2) [[11]]

## Todo

See [Foramt details](README.md#todo-format)

- Initial design [[1]]
- PoC implementation of a manager
- Explore `linkme` / `inventory` for a benchmark harness on top of
  `tprobe` — self-registering benchmarks (no `main()` edits per
  benchmark); possibly reusable for actor registration too [[9]]
```

```
1	//! Runtimes that drive [`Actor`]s. Two flavors live here:
2	//! [`SingleThreadRuntime`] (Goal1: one thread, FIFO queue) and
3	//! [`MultiThreadRuntime`] (Goal2: one thread per actor, `mpsc`
4	//! channels).
5	
6	use std::collections::VecDeque;
7	use std::sync::mpsc::{self, Receiver, Sender};
8	use std::thread::{self, JoinHandle};
9	use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
10	
11	use crate::{Actor, Context, Message};
12	use tprobe::TProbe;
13	
14	/// Single-threaded runtime: all actors run on the calling thread,
15	/// sharing one FIFO message queue. Dispatch loops until either the
16	/// caller-supplied deadline expires or the queue drains.
17	///
18	/// Dispatch is wrapped in a [`TProbe`] batched scope covering
19	/// `inner` consecutive `handle_message` calls; per-event latency
20	/// is available via [`Self::probe_mut`] after [`Self::run_for`]
21	/// returns. The scope's `site_id` is the first `dst_id` in the
22	/// batch (arbitrary-but-stable when `inner > 1`).
23	pub struct SingleThreadRuntime {
24	    actors: Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>,
25	    queue: VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,
26	    probe: TProbe,
27	}
28	
29	impl SingleThreadRuntime {
30	    /// Create an empty runtime with no actors or pending messages.
31	    /// `probe_name` names the embedded probe in its band-table
32	    /// report (e.g. `"goal1.dispatch"`).
33	    pub fn new(probe_name: &str) -> Self {
34	        Self {
35	            actors: Vec::new(),
36	            queue: VecDeque::new(),
37	            probe: TProbe::new(probe_name),
38	        }
39	    }
40	
41	    /// Register `actor`; the returned `u32` is its stable id for
42	    /// the lifetime of the runtime and is usable as `dst_id` in
43	    /// [`Context::send`]. Ids are allocated sequentially from 0.
44	    pub fn add_actor(&mut self, actor: Box<dyn Actor>) -> u32 {
45	        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;
46	        self.actors.push(actor);
47	        id
48	    }
49	
50	    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`. Used to prime the
51	    /// dispatch loop before [`Self::run_for`].
52	    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {
53	        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));
54	    }
55	
56	    /// Dispatch messages until `duration` elapses or the queue
57	    /// empties. Each probe scope wraps `inner` consecutive
58	    /// dispatches. A partial trailing batch (queue emptied
59	    /// mid-scope) is discarded — neither recorded nor counted —
60	    /// so throughput numbers reflect whole-batch work only.
61	    ///
62	    /// Returns the total number of messages counted toward
63	    /// whole batches.
64	    pub fn run_for(&mut self, duration: Duration, inner: u64) -> u64 {
65	        let deadline = Instant::now() + duration;
66	        let mut count = 0u64;
67	        while Instant::now() < deadline {
68	            let first_dst = match self.queue.front() {
69	                Some((d, _)) => *d,
70	                None => break,
71	            };
72	            let Self {
73	                actors,
74	                queue,
75	                probe,
76	            } = self;
77	            let id = probe.start(first_dst as u64);
78	            let mut done: u64 = 0;
79	            while done < inner {
80	                let Some((dst, msg)) = queue.pop_front() else {
81	                    break;
82	                };
83	                let actor = &mut actors[dst as usize];
84	                let mut ctx = SingleCtx { queue };
85	                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);
86	                done += 1;
87	            }
88	            if done == inner {
89	                probe.end_batch(id, inner);
90	                count += done;
91	            } else {
92	                // Partial trailing batch: drop the scope (don't
93	                // record or count) and stop.
94	                break;
95	            }
96	        }
97	        count
98	    }
99	
100	    /// Mutable access to the embedded probe — used by callers to
101	    /// invoke [`TProbe::clear`] at the warmup → measurement
102	    /// boundary and [`TProbe::report`] after [`Self::run_for`]
103	    /// returns.
104	    pub fn probe_mut(&mut self) -> &mut TProbe {
105	        &mut self.probe
106	    }
107	}
108	
109	/// [`Context`] impl used by [`SingleThreadRuntime`]: pushes onto
110	/// the runtime's shared queue.
111	struct SingleCtx<'a> {
112	    queue: &'a mut VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,
113	}
114	
115	impl Context for SingleCtx<'_> {
116	    /// Push `(dst_id, msg)` onto the shared dispatch queue.
117	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {
118	        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));
119	    }
120	}

131	    }

133	    /// Bounded ping-pong actor: replies to its peer up to `max`
134	    /// times, then goes silent. Used to produce a deterministic
135	    /// message count in unit tests without wall-clock dependence.

137	        peer: u32,

141	
142	    impl Actor for Bouncer {
143	        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
144	            if self.sent < self.max {
145	                ctx.send(self.peer, Message);
146	                self.sent += 1;
147	            }
148	        }
149	    }

171	            max: 5,
172	        }));
173	        rt.seed(a, Message);
174	        // seed (1) + 5 from a + 5 from b = 11, all whole batches at inner=1.
175	        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 1);
176	        assert_eq!(n, 11);
177	    }

182	        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 0);

190	        // counted (2) and one partial (1) discarded.
191	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.partial");
192	        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
193	            peer: 1,

196	        }));

198	            peer: 0,
199	            sent: 0,
200	            max: 1,
201	        }));
202	        rt.seed(a, Message);
203	        // Events: seed→b, b→a (b.sent=1), a→b (a.sent=1), b stops.
204	        // 3 dispatches total. inner=2 ⇒ first batch of 2 recorded
205	        // (count=2), then partial batch of 1 discarded.
206	        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 2);
207	        assert_eq!(n, 2);
208	    }
```
