  "new_string": "- **Future benchmark-crate warmup helper.** When we build\n  a benchmark harness (see the linkme/inventory idea\n  captured at 0.3.0-0), the harness will need a warmup\n  helper since it calls `calibrate()` without an\n  app-level dispatch loop to warm the CPU. The bot thinks\n  the right home is `tprobe` — probably a new\n  `tprobe::warmup` module with a `cpu_boost(iterations)`\n  or `cpu_boost(Duration)` helper — since tprobe already\n  owns the measurement primitives and warmup is a\n  sibling concern. Tracked in `notes/todo.md`.\n\n## Lower `--warmup` default 10 s → 0.5 s (0.3.0-5)\n\nChanges the `--warmup` default on `goal1` and `goal2` from\n`10.0` seconds to `0.5` seconds. The 10 s default dominated\nwall-clock time for short iterative runs (e.g.\n`goal1 1 --inner 10` spent 91 % of its total time in\nwarmup), which was an ergonomic trap — users assumed\ncalibration was slow when it was actually the warmup. The\nnew 0.5 s default lets `goal1 <seconds>` and\n`goal2 <seconds>` run for roughly `seconds + 0.5 + ~0.01`\nwall-clock, which matches what the CLI suggests. Users who\nwant long warmup for reference-quality measurement can pass\n`--warmup 10` explicitly.\n\nAt 0.5 s the CPU is typically at or very near its boost\nfrequency and caches/branch-predictors are primed for the\ndispatch loop. `tprobe`'s `calibrate()` runs immediately\nafter and finishes in ~10 ms, so 0.5 s remains ample\nheadroom for the steady-state conditions calibration\nassumes (see `crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`\n\"Calibration preconditions\").\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`,\n  `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`:\n  `#[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0, ...)]` →\n  `default_value_t = 0.5`.\n- `README.md`: `--warmup` bullet default line updated\n  (`default 10.0` → `default 0.5`); usage guidance reworded\n  to recommend `--warmup 10` for reference measurements and\n  `--warmup 0` for fastest iteration. Other stale samples /\n  narrative still get their full refresh at the `0.3.0`\n  closing marker.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **0.5 s, not a proportional fraction.** Earlier discussion\n  considered `min(duration / 10, 2 s)`-style proportional\n  warmup, but a fixed small default is simpler, predictable\n  across durations, and cheap enough even for 0.1 s runs\n  (warmup is ~5× the measurement, but total wall-clock is\n  still sub-second). Users who want proportional behavior\n  can script it; defaulting to a fixed 0.5 s gives a single\n  number to remember.\n- **0.5 s rather than 0.1 s or 1.0 s.** 0.1 s is cutting\n  the CPU-settle margin too close for some systems; 1.0 s\n  starts feeling slow on `goal1 0.5` type runs (warmup\n  dominates again). 0.5 s is a pragmatic middle: long\n  enough to settle, short enough to disappear in the\n  terminal.\n- **No alias or `--fast`-style flag added.** Setting\n  `--warmup 0` is already the \"fastest\" mode; adding a\n  separate flag for the same effect would be noise. Users\n  who want zero-warmup explicitly can pass it; the default\n  just stops penalizing them by default.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"
}
```

All clean:

- `goal1 --help` shows `-w/--warmup`, `-i/--inner`, `-t/--ticks`, `-p/--pin`
- `goal2 --help` shows `-w/--warmup`, `-t/--ticks`, `-p/--pin` (no `-i` since goal2 has no `--inner`)
- fmt / clippy / 23 tests all clean
- `goal1 0.1 --inner 1000` runtime dropped from ~10.1 s → ~0.635 s with the new default
- All short aliases resolve; help is readable

**Summary of 0.3.0-5 (expanded with short aliases):**

- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.0-4 → 0.3.0-5`.
- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`, `goal2.rs`:
  - `--warmup` default `10.0 → 0.5`.
  - Added `short` aliases: `-w/--warmup` + `-p/--pin` on both, plus `-i/--inner` on goal1.
- `README.md`: warmup default line updated; usage guidance reworded; flag bullets gain short-alias prefixes matching the pre-existing `-t / --ticks` style.
- `notes/chores-01.md`: new `## Lower --warmup default 10 s → 0.5 s (0.3.0-5)` section with four design decisions (0.5 s vs proportional; 0.5 vs 0.1/1.0; no `--fast` alias; short flags folded in here).
- `notes/todo.md`: `0.3.0-4` → Done; `0.3.0-5` → In Progress; reference `[14]` added.

Proposed commit:

**Title**
```
feat: warmup default 10→0.5, short flag aliases (0.3.0-5)
```

**Body**
```
Lowers the `--warmup` default from 10.0 s to 0.5 s on both
`goal1` and `goal2`. The 10 s default was an ergonomic trap
for short iterative runs — `goal1 1` spent 10.5 s in
warmup for 1 s of measurement, which users routinely
mistook for slow calibration. 0.5 s is still comfortably
past the CPU freq-boost settle point (calibration runs
~10 ms right after, with room to spare) and users who
want reference-quality warmup can pass `--warmup 10`
explicitly.

Also adds short-flag aliases for the other common flags,
matching the pre-existing `-t/--ticks` pattern: `-w/--warmup`
and `-p/--pin` on both binaries, plus `-i/--inner` on
goal1. Makes interactive iteration terser
(`goal1 1 -w 0 -i 1000 -p 0`).

- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`,

```
40	
41	## Usage
42	
43	Stage 1 (the PoC landed at 0.1.0) provides two binaries:
44	
45	- **`goal1`** — two actors on one thread, ping-ponging a
46	  message through a shared `VecDeque`.
47	- **`goal2`** — two actors on two threads, ping-ponging over
48	  `std::sync::mpsc` channels.
49	
50	Install with `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1`; both share
51	a common CLI shape:
52	
53	```
54	$ goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1000 --pin 0
55	actor-x1 0.2.0
56	goal1: 102087000 messages in 0.500s (204.174 M msg/s, inner=1000)
57	  pinning: main → core 0
58	  apparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 0 tk (0.00 ns)
59	
60	  tprobe: goal1.dispatch [count=102,087]
61	              first     last    range        count mean
62	    min-p1        0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
63	    p1-p10        0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
64	    p10-p20       4 ns     4 ns     0 ns    32,684    4 ns
65	    p20-p30       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
66	    p30-p40       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
67	    p40-p50       5 ns     5 ns     0 ns    30,128    5 ns
68	    p50-p60       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
69	    p60-p70       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
70	    p70-p80       5 ns     5 ns     0 ns    34,218    5 ns
71	    p80-p90       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns
72	    p90-p99       5 ns     7 ns     2 ns     3,971    6 ns
73	    p99-max       7 ns    34 ns    27 ns     1,086    9 ns
74	    mean                                              5 ns
75	    stdev                                             1 ns
76	    mean min-p99                                      5 ns
77	    stdev min-p99                                     0 ns
78	```
79	
80	### Flags
81	
82	- **`<duration_s>`** — positional, required; measurement window
83	  in seconds.
84	- **`-w` / `--warmup <SECS>`** — default 0.5; run the same
85	  dispatch loop with the probe active for this long, then clear
86	  the probe at the boundary so only steady-state data reaches
87	  the report. Set to 0 for quickest iteration; raise to 10 s or
88	  more for reference measurements where you want the CPU fully
89	  settled.
90	- **`-i` / `--inner <N>`** *(goal1 only)* — default 1, ≥1;
91	  dispatches per probe scope. Larger values amortize the probe's
92	  two-`rdtsc` apparatus cost and give the histogram meaningful
93	  dynamic range. goal2 fixes `inner=1` because its per-channel
94	  queue holds at most one message.
95	- **`-t` / `--ticks`** — display probe values as raw ticks
96	  instead of nanoseconds.
97	- **`-p` / `--pin <CORES>`** — pin threads to logical CPUs. goal1 uses
98	  the first core in the list; goal2 pins actor `i` to
99	  `cores[i % len]`. Accepts comma lists and ranges: `--pin 5`,
100	  `--pin 0,1`, `--pin 0-3`, `--pin 0,0` (oversubscribe).
101	
102	### Typical invocations
103	
104	```
105	# Short iterative smoke, no warmup:
106	goal1 0.1 --warmup 0 --inner 1000
107	
108	# Full measurement with pinning:
109	goal1 2 --inner 1000 --pin 0
110	
111	# Goal2 with actors on separate cores (same complex):
112	goal2 1 --pin 0,1
113	```
114	
115	## Reading the band table
116	
117	The `tprobe` block is a 12-row percentile histogram with four
118	summary rows. Every row renders every run, even when a band
119	holds no data — a zero row is distinguishable from a missing row.
120	
121	### Header
122	
123	```
124	tprobe: <name> [count=N]
125	```
126	
127	`<name>` identifies the probe (`goal1.dispatch`,
128	`goal2.dispatch.actor0`, …). `count=N` is the number of **scopes**
129	drained into the histogram, not total dispatches — at `--inner
130	1000` one scope covers 1,000 dispatches, so `count` here is
131	`(total_messages / inner)`. Total dispatch count is in the summary
132	line above.
133	
134	### Rows
135	
136	Twelve percentile bands in order: `min-p1`, `p1-p10`, …, `p90-p99`,
137	`p99-max`. Each row covers the samples whose rank in the sorted
138	distribution falls within that percentile range.
139	
140	### Columns
141	
142	- **first** — lowest value observed in the band.
143	- **last** — highest value observed in the band.
144	- **range** — `last - first + 1`; width of the band's value range.
145	- **count** — number of samples in the band.
146	- **mean** — average value within the band.
147	
148	### Summary rows
149	
150	- **mean / stdev** — whole-histogram statistics, including the
151	  `p99-max` tail.
152	- **mean min-p99 / stdev min-p99** — same statistics excluding
153	  `p99-max`. Useful for A/B-ing code changes because the tail can
154	  be dominated by rare outliers (OS preemption, lock contention,
155	  interrupts) that drown signal in the body of the distribution.
156	
157	### Why a band can be all zeros
158	
159	The renderer walks `hdrhistogram` buckets and assigns each bucket
160	to one band by its cumulative mid-rank. A tight distribution has
161	most samples in a handful of buckets, and each bucket lands
162	entirely in one band — so neighboring bands show zeros even
163	though they'd nominally hold ~10% of the data. It's a display
164	artifact, not missing data: the samples are visible in the
165	adjacent non-zero band. A `stdev min-p99` near 0 alongside a
166	couple of dense bands is a strong signal that the underlying
167	distribution is spike-heavy.
168	
169	### The `apparatus:` line
170	
171	```
172	apparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 0 tk (0.00 ns)
173	```
174	
175	Reports the fitted overhead model. `framing` is the hardware
176	floor cost of the probe's two-`rdtsc` pair, measured by a
177	two-point fit on empty `black_box(1)` scopes. `per-event at
178	inner=N` is what the report subtracts from each stored value —
179	`framing / N`, which amortizes toward zero as `inner` grows.
180	Under `--raw` this line reads `raw (no overhead subtraction)`
181	and calibration is skipped.
182	
183	### The `pinning:` line
184	
185	```
186	pinning: actor0→core0, actor1→core4
187	```
188	
189	Shows what `--pin` produced for each workload thread;
190	`none (unpinned)` means the OS is free to schedule threads
191	anywhere.
192	
193	## The bot's understanding
194	
195	A handful of recurring patterns in the output, with the bot's
196	best read of why the numbers look the way they do. Specific
197	numbers below are from an AMD Ryzen 3900X at ~3.7 GHz — compare
198	your own numbers to your own baseline, not to these.
199	
200	### goal1 `--inner 1` shows ~9 ns; `--inner 1000` shows ~5 ns
201	
202	The probe's two-`rdtsc` framing has a real cost (~4 ns on modern
203	x86) that contaminates every stored scope delta. At `inner=1`
204	the full framing lands on every sample, pushing the reported
205	per-event cost to ~9 ns; at `inner=1000` framing is amortized
206	(~0.004 ns per event), so the stored value converges on the
207	actual `handle_message` cost of ~5 ns. `--raw` makes this
208	concrete — `--raw --inner 1` reads ~9 ns, `--raw --inner 1000`
209	reads ~5 ns. Default overhead calibration subtracts the framing,
210	so adjusted `--inner 1` also lands at ~5 ns, matching
211	`--inner 1000`.
212	
213	### goal2 is ~900× slower than goal1 `--inner 1000`
214	
215	goal1 `--inner 1000`: ~5 ns per event (~200 M msg/s).
216	goal2 `--inner 1`: ~1–1.5 µs per event (~0.25 M msg/s).
217	The bot thinks the gap is dominated by:
218	
219	- **`mpsc`'s `Mutex` + condvar wake** on every send and recv —
220	  even a fast bounded mpsc pays this on every cross-thread
221	  hand-off.
222	- **Two context switches per round-trip** — one per channel
223	  hand-off, so a ping-pong cycle of (A send → B recv → B send →
224	  A recv) crosses the scheduler four times.
225	- **Cache-line bouncing** — the shared channel metadata and the
226	  message migrate between the two actors' L1 caches each round.
227	
228	An unbounded lock-free mpsc would close some of this; the
229	context-switch floor is fundamental to any "block, wake, run"
230	channel pattern.
231	
232	### `--pin` tightens `stdev` but can raise the mean
233	
234	Pinning eliminates OS thread-migration noise — a thread landing
235	on a different core than the previous message's cache
236	warmed — so `stdev min-p99` drops (on this box: 246 ns → 80 ns,
237	about 3× tighter). But pinning also *forces* placement, which
238	can be worse than what the OS would have chosen. On this 3900X
239	(12 cores split across 4 CCX complexes of 3 cores each),
240	`--pin 0,4` puts the two actors on different CCX complexes, so
241	every ping-pong round forces a cross-complex cache coherence
242	transfer — the mean rises from ~630 ns to ~1,500 ns. `--pin 0,1`
243	(same CCX) keeps the mean near unpinned while still tightening
244	the stdev.
245	
246	This isn't a flaw; it's the intended trade. Pinning gives you
247	control over what you measure — pick cores that match the
248	scenario you care about (best-case same-complex, worst-case
249	cross-socket, etc.).
250	
251	### The `p99-max` tail occasionally spikes to tens of µs or ms
252	
253	Almost always an OS interrupt, scheduler preemption, or a rare
254	lock contention sneaking into a probe scope. The probe sees real
255	events the system experiences, so the measurement is honest —
256	they just aren't what you typically want to compare across
257	runs. `mean min-p99` excludes this tail and is usually the
258	right comparator for A/B work.
259	
260	### Numbers travel with the hardware
261	
262	- **Framing** is ~20–50 ticks regardless of clock speed
263	  (pipeline cost in cycles, not time).
264	- **goal1 per-event cost at large `inner`** scales with clock
265	  frequency.
266	- **goal2 latency** depends heavily on CPU topology (single
267	  socket vs NUMA), cache architecture, and scheduler tuning.
268	
269	If the numbers on your box differ from those above, that's
```

  "old_string": "### Columns\n\n- **first** — lowest value observed in the band.\n- **last** — highest value observed in the band.\n- **range** — `last - first + 1`; width of the band's value range.\n- **count** — number of samples in the band.\n- **mean** — average value within the band.\n\n### Summary rows\n\n- **mean / stdev** — whole-histogram statistics, including the\n  `p99-max` tail.\n- **mean min-p99 / stdev min-p99** — same statistics excluding\n  `p99-max`. Useful for A/B-ing code changes because the tail can\n  be dominated by rare outliers (OS preemption, lock contention,\n  interrupts) that drown signal in the body of the distribution.",
  "new_string": "### Columns\n\n- **first** — lowest value observed in the band.\n- **last** — highest value observed in the band.\n- **range** — `last - first + 1`; width of the band's value range.\n- **count** — number of samples in the band.\n- **mean** — average value within the band. Raw, unadjusted —\n  same hardware-tick deltas the probe recorded.\n- **adj mean** — same mean, minus the per-event apparatus\n  correction from calibration (clamped at 0). See the\n  `apparatus:` line for the correction amount and\n  [`crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`](crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md)\n  for the formal model.\n\n### Summary rows\n\n- **mean / stdev** — whole-histogram statistics, including the\n  `p99-max` tail. `adj mean` on the `mean` row applies the\n  per-event correction the same way as the band rows; `stdev`\n  leaves the `adj mean` column blank because standard deviation\n  is shift-invariant.\n- **mean min-p99 / stdev min-p99** — same statistics excluding\n  `p99-max`. Useful for A/B-ing code changes because the tail can\n  be dominated by rare outliers (OS preemption, lock contention,\n  interrupts) that drown signal in the body of the distribution.\n  Same raw-vs-adj treatment as the `mean` / `stdev` pair."
}

  "old_string": "### The `apparatus:` line\n\n```\napparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 0 tk (0.00 ns)\n```\n\nReports the fitted overhead model. `framing` is the hardware\nfloor cost of the probe's two-`rdtsc` pair, measured by a\ntwo-point fit on empty `black_box(1)` scopes. `per-event at\ninner=N` is what the report subtracts from each stored value —\n`framing / N`, which amortizes toward zero as `inner` grows.\nUnder `--raw` this line reads `raw (no overhead subtraction)`\nand calibration is skipped.",
  "new_string": "### The `apparatus:` line\n\n```\napparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); loop_per_iter=0.93 tk (0.24 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 1 tk (0.26 ns)\n```\n\nReports the fitted overhead model from calibration.\n\n- **`framing`** — hardware floor cost of the probe's two-`rdtsc`\n  pair, paid once per scope. Measured as the intercept of a\n  two-point fit on empty `black_box(1)` scopes.\n- **`loop_per_iter`** — per-iteration cost of the calibration's\n  empty inner loop (one `black_box(1)` per iter). Measured as\n  the slope of the same fit.\n- **`per-event at inner=N`** — the per-event correction shown in\n  the band-table's `adj mean` column: `framing / N +\n  loop_per_iter`, rounded to the nearest tick. Amortizes toward\n  `loop_per_iter` as `inner` grows.\n\nCalibration runs unconditionally (~10 ms wall-clock); the\nhistogram still stores raw per-event values, and this correction\nis applied only in the `adj mean` display column. See\n[`crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`](crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md)\nfor the derivation and the unmeasurable-overhead category."
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/README.md",
  "old_string": "### goal1 `--inner 1` shows ~9 ns; `--inner 1000` shows ~5 ns\n\nThe probe's two-`rdtsc` framing has a real cost (~4 ns on modern\nx86) that contaminates every stored scope delta. At `inner=1`\nthe full framing lands on every sample, pushing the reported\nper-event cost to ~9 ns; at `inner=1000` framing is amortized\n(~0.004 ns per event), so the stored value converges on the\nactual `handle_message` cost of ~5 ns. `--raw` makes this\nconcrete — `--raw --inner 1` reads ~9 ns, `--raw --inner 1000`\nreads ~5 ns. Default overhead calibration subtracts the framing,\nso adjusted `--inner 1` also lands at ~5 ns, matching\n`--inner 1000`.",
  "new_string": "### goal1 raw vs `adj mean`, and how `inner` changes it\n\nThe probe's two-`rdtsc` framing has a real cost (~5 ns on modern\nx86) that contaminates every stored scope delta — the histogram\nstores this raw value, so the `mean` column shows it. At\n`inner=1` the full framing lands on every per-event sample,\npushing `mean` to ~10 ns; the `adj mean` column applies the\n`framing + loop_per_iter` correction and reports ~4 ns —\napproximately the true `handle_message` cost. At `inner=1000`\nframing is amortized to ~0.02 ns per event, so `mean` and\n`adj mean` both land at ~5 ns (they agree because the correction\nis tiny at this batch size). The two regimes converge on the\nsame work-alone cost, just via different paths: low `inner`\nrelies on the correction, high `inner` shows it directly."
}
```

`CAL_WARMUP` loop removed; `calibrate()` documents caller-warmup contract |\n| `0.3.0-5`   | `--warmup` default 10 s → 0.5 s; `-w/-i/-p` short aliases |\n| **`0.3.0`** | **Closing marker**                                        |\n\n### What ships in 0.3.0\n\n- Band-table report with raw hardware-tick columns on the\n  left (`first` / `last` / `range` / `count` / `mean`) plus\n  a rightmost `adj mean` column showing the\n  apparatus-corrected value. `stdev` rows leave the `adj`\n  column blank (shift-invariant). 8-space separator between\n  `mean` and `adj mean` so the raw-vs-derived distinction\n  is visually obvious.\n- Overhead subtraction model extended beyond framing to\n  include `loop_per_iter_ticks` (amortized across `batch`).\n  Correction is averaged across drained records and passed\n  to the renderer — histogram stores raw values unchanged.\n- `tprobe` at `0.1.3` — standalone crate providing\n  `TProbe` / `TProbeRecId`, `Overhead` / `calibrate`, and\n  the `band_table` / `fmt` / `pin` / `ticks` submodules.\n  Calibration assumes a caller-warmed CPU; See\n   [`crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md`](../crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md)\n   \"Stage2 runtime\" section.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"

> AGENT

Ladder closed. 0.3.0 is on `main`.

> DEVELOPER

I wonder if we should have a digit or 2 right of a decimal point when doing time

> AGENT

Good UX observation. The current output uses zero decimals everywhere, which hides sub-ns detail and is the reason `range=1 tk` and `p1-p10` clusters render as `0 ns` even though they're real. Tradeoff is column width and "every tight row gets `.0` noise."

A few concrete shapes:

**(a) 1 decimal on ns columns, keep ticks integer.**
```
    min-p1        0.0 ns     0.0 ns     0.0 ns         0    0.0 ns        0.0 ns
    p1-p10        4.2 ns     4.2 ns     0.3 ns    18,076    4.2 ns        4.2 ns
    p99-max       7.1 ns    54.2 ns    46.8 ns     1,072    8.9 ns        8.9 ns
```
Minimal width increase (+2 chars per cell). Sub-ns detail visible. Integer ticks stay clean.

**(b) 2 decimals on ns, keep ticks integer.**
```
    p1-p10        4.25 ns     4.25 ns     0.26 ns    18,076    4.25 ns        4.25 ns
```
More precise, +3 chars per cell, feels dense for goal2's 1,000+ values.

**(c) Adaptive: 2 decimals if value < 10, 1 if < 100, 0 otherwise (ns only).**
```
    p1-p10        4.25 ns     4.25 ns     0.26 ns    18,076    4.25 ns        4.25 ns
    p90-p99       5.32 ns    6.45 ns     1.14 ns     5,644    6.12 ns        6.08 ns
    ...
    goal2: 1,136 ns    1,323 ns    ...     1,228    1,231 ns        1,215 ns
```
Best-of-both: small values show detail, large values stay tidy. Slight code complexity in `fmt_commas_f64` call sites (or a new helper).

**(d) Keep integer, tell readers to use `-t` for precision.** Status quo. No code change.

My lean is **(a)** — one decimal is the smallest change that fixes the "0 ns" mystery for real, and `5.0 ns` is mildly noisy but not wrong. (c) is better UX but costs a helper or inline match.

Which direction, and want me to fold it into the existing `range` / `destructure` cleanup todo, or make it its own item since it touches the output format?

> DEVELOPER

I like (a) too, I assume "easy" change and we can see how it looks

See\n   [`crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md`](../crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md)\n   \"Stage2 runtime\" section.\n\n## Band-table ns-mode shows 1 decimal (0.3.1)\n\nChanges `band_table::render` to format ns-mode cells with\none decimal place (`\"4.5 ns\"`) instead of rounding to\ninteger nanoseconds (`\"5 ns\"`). Ticks mode is unchanged —\ninteger counts are already precise. Resolves a long-\nstanding readability issue where sub-ns detail was\nsilently rounded away:\n\n- 1 hardware tick ≈ 0.26 ns on a 3.7 GHz box, so OoO-\n  coalesced `rdtsc` pairs and hdrhistogram's tightest\n  buckets (`range=1 tk`) rendered as `\"0 ns\"`, masking\n  real data.\n- Adjacent histogram buckets that hold distinct but\n  close values (e.g. 17 tk vs 18 tk at inner=1000)\n  collapsed to the same display (`\"5 ns\"`) despite being\n  resolvable in the underlying data.\n\nWith one decimal the distinction becomes visible:\n`\"4.5 ns\"` vs `\"4.7 ns\"` vs `\"5.0 ns\"` on the same hot-\ncluster region, and the p1-p10 OoO cluster that used to\nrender `0 ns` now shows `0.3 ns` — more honest.\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.0` → `0.3.1`.\n- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.3` → `0.1.4` — render\n  output format changed.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`: new local\n  `let decimals = if as_ticks { 0 } else { 1 };`\n  threaded through every `fmt_commas_f64` call in\n  `render()` (band rows, summary-mean/stdev strings,\n  trimmed-mean/stdev strings). Inline comment documents\n  the rationale. `cargo fmt` reformatted one\n  `hist_mean_adj_str` call site into multi-line form\n  since it crossed the width limit.\n- `README.md`: `## Usage` sample-output block regenerated\n  with the 1-decimal format.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **One decimal, not two.** Two decimals (`\"4.25 ns\"`)\n  surface slightly more detail but widen every ns cell by\n  another character, which crowds goal2's wide band-table\n  (values in the thousands of ns). One decimal is the\n  smallest change that resolves the \"1 tk → 0 ns\" masking\n  issue and keeps columns readable.\n- **Ticks mode stays integer.** Integer tick counts are\n  already exact — adding decimals would inject spurious\n  `\".0\"` noise without carrying any information. `-t` is\n  the right mode when sub-tick resolution matters\n  (there's no such thing as sub-tick).\n- **Single-step `0.3.1` patch release.** Mechanical,\n  local, single display concern — doesn't need the `-N`\n  ladder treatment. Follows `CLAUDE.md`'s \"single-step\n  recommended for mechanical changes\" guidance.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"

> AGENT

Clean: fmt/clippy/23 tests all pass. Output previews:

**inner=1000 ns:** `4.5 / 4.7 / 5.0 ns` hot-cluster split is now visible (was all `5 ns`); `range 0.3 ns` shows the 1-tick bucket width.

**inner=1 ns:** p1-p10 OoO cluster shows `0.3 ns` (was `0 ns` mystery); p60-p70 hot bucket `10.0 ns → adj 1.8 ns`.

**goal2:** sub-ns detail survives at hundreds-of-ns scale (`671.5 ns`, `1,617.5 ns`); columns widen a bit but still fit.

**ticks mode:** unchanged, still integer.

Proposed commit:

**Title**
```
feat: 1-decimal ns display in band-table (0.3.1)
```

**Body**
```
Changes band-table render to show ns-mode cells with one
decimal place (e.g. "4.5 ns") instead of integer ns
("5 ns"). Ticks mode unchanged — integer tick counts are
already exact. Resolves a long-standing readability issue
where sub-ns detail was silently rounded away: 1 hardware
tick ≈ 0.26 ns on a 3.7 GHz box, so OoO-coalesced `rdtsc`
pairs and tightest hdrhistogram buckets (`range=1 tk`)
rendered as "0 ns", masking real data.

- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.3 → 0.1.4` — render
  output format changed.
- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`: new local
  `let decimals = if as_ticks { 0 } else { 1 };` threaded
  through every `fmt_commas_f64` call in `render()`.
  Inline comment documents the rationale. `cargo fmt`
  reformatted one long `hist_mean_adj_str` call site
  into multi-line form.
- `README.md`: `## Usage` sample-output block regenerated
  with 1-decimal format.
- `notes/chores-01.md`: new chore section with three
  design decisions (1 decimal not 2; ticks stays integer;
  single-step patch release).
- `notes/todo.md`: new Done entry with reference `[16]`.
```

OK to run `vc-x1 push main`?

> DEVELOPER

how hard to let the user decide?

> AGENT

Not hard — ~30 lines of mechanical plumbing across 4 files.

**Design** (quickest shape):

Add a CLI flag `--decimals <N>` (short `-d`) on both goal1 and goal2. Default: no value → smart default (`0` in ticks mode, `1` in ns mode — today's behavior). Explicit: overrides both modes.

- `goal1 0.5 -i 1000 -p 0` → 1 decimal ns (today).
- `goal1 0.5 -i 1000 -p 0 -t` → 0 decimal ticks.
- `goal1 0.5 -i 1000 -p 0 -d 2` → 2 decimal ns.
- `goal1 0.5 -i 1000 -p 0 -t -d 3` → 3 decimal ticks (weird, but honest).

**Plumbing:**

- `Cli { decimals: Option<usize> }` on goal1 + goal2 (clap accepts `Option<usize>` natively).
- `TProbe::report` signature gains `decimals: Option<usize>` → forwarded to render. Public API change → `tprobe` version bumps (we'd already be going 0.1.3 → 0.1.4 for the default change).
- `band_table::render` gains `decimals: Option<usize>` → resolves to `decimals.unwrap_or(if as_ticks { 0 } else { 1 })` at the top of the function.

**Scope choice:**

(a) **Fold into 0.3.1.** The default-1-decimal change *and* the override flag land together. One commit, one version bump. Small extra scope but the two concerns travel nicely together.

(b) **Ship 0.3.1 as the default-1-decimal change; add the flag as 0.3.2.** Keeps each step focused. Costs a second push cycle, marginal.

I'd lean (a) — they're the same code path and the flag is trivial enough that separating them is more friction than value.

Which do you prefer?

> DEVELOPER

add to 0.3.1

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Render a band-table report for `hist`, interpreting stored\n/// values as hardware ticks (raw — no overhead subtraction is\n/// applied to histogram-stored values). `kind` is the header\n/// label (e.g. `\"tprobe\"`) and `name` is the probe's name.\n/// `as_ticks=false` converts to ns; `true` keeps raw ticks.\n///\n/// `correction` (in ticks) is the per-event overhead to display\n/// in an `adj mean` column alongside the raw mean. If `Some(c)`,\n/// each band's / summary row's raw mean has `c` subtracted\n/// (clamped at 0) for the `adj mean` column. `stdev` rows leave\n/// that column blank — stdev is shift-invariant. If `None`, no\n/// adj column is rendered and the output matches the pre-0.3.0\n/// layout.\npub(crate) fn render(\n    kind: &str,\n    name: &str,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    as_ticks: bool,\n    correction: Option<u64>,\n) {\n    let sample_count = hist.len();\n    println!(\"  {kind}: {name} [count={}]\", fmt_commas(sample_count));\n    if sample_count == 0 {\n        println!();\n        return;\n    }\n\n    let unit = if as_ticks { \"tk\" } else { \"ns\" };\n    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n    let conv = |v: u64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v as f64 } else { v as f64 / tpn } };\n    let conv_f = |v: f64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v } else { v / tpn } };\n    // Integer ticks are already precise; ns mode loses sub-ns\n    // resolution at zero decimals (e.g. 1 tk ≈ 0.26 ns renders\n    // as \"0 ns\"), so show one decimal in ns mode.\n    let decimals = if as_ticks { 0 } else { 1 };\n    let correction_ticks_f = correction.map(|c| c as f64).unwrap_or(0.0);\n    let show_adj = correction.is_some();",
  "new_string": "/// Render a band-table report for `hist`, interpreting stored\n/// values as hardware ticks (raw — no overhead subtraction is\n/// applied to histogram-stored values). `kind` is the header\n/// label (e.g. `\"tprobe\"`) and `name` is the probe's name.\n/// `as_ticks=false` converts to ns; `true` keeps raw ticks.\n///\n/// `correction` (in ticks) is the per-event overhead to display\n/// in an `adj mean` column alongside the raw mean. If `Some(c)`,\n/// each band's / summary row's raw mean has `c` subtracted\n/// (clamped at 0) for the `adj mean` column. `stdev` rows leave\n/// that column blank — stdev is shift-invariant. If `None`, no\n/// adj column is rendered and the output matches the pre-0.3.0\n/// layout.\n///\n/// `decimals` controls the fractional precision of numeric\n/// cells. `None` uses the smart default: `0` in ticks mode\n/// (integers are already exact), `1` in ns mode (so sub-ns\n/// detail is visible rather than rounded away). `Some(n)`\n/// overrides both modes.\npub(crate) fn render(\n    kind: &str,\n    name: &str,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    as_ticks: bool,\n    correction: Option<u64>,\n    decimals: Option<usize>,\n) {\n    let sample_count = hist.len();\n    println!(\"  {kind}: {name} [count={}]\", fmt_commas(sample_count));\n    if sample_count == 0 {\n        println!();\n        return;\n    }\n\n    let unit = if as_ticks { \"tk\" } else { \"ns\" };\n    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n    let conv = |v: u64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v as f64 } else { v as f64 / tpn } };\n    let conv_f = |v: f64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v } else { v / tpn } };\n    // Smart default: integer ticks are already precise, ns loses\n    // sub-ns resolution at 0 decimals (1 tk ≈ 0.26 ns renders as\n    // \"0 ns\"), so ns defaults to 1 decimal. Explicit `--decimals N`\n    // overrides.\n    let decimals = decimals.unwrap_or(if as_ticks { 0 } else { 1 });\n    let correction_ticks_f = correction.map(|c| c as f64).unwrap_or(0.0);\n    let show_adj = correction.is_some();"
}
```

If\n    /// `overhead` is `Some`, the per-event correction is\n    /// averaged across drained records and passed to\n    /// `band_table::render`, which displays it in an `adj mean`\n    /// column alongside the raw columns. See\n    /// `notes/overhead-model.md`.\n    pub fn report(&mut self, as_ticks: bool, overhead: Option<&Overhead>) {",
  "new_string": "    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`\n    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick\n    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows\n    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`). `decimals` controls the\n    /// fractional precision of numeric cells — `None` uses a\n    /// mode-aware default (`0` for ticks, `1` for ns); `Some(n)`\n    /// overrides.\n    ///\n    /// Drains any pending `start` / `end*` records into the\n    /// histogram as **raw** per-event values:\n    /// `per_event_raw = (end_tsc − start_tsc) / batch`, clamped\n    /// to `1` since the histogram lower bound is 1. If\n    /// `overhead` is `Some`, the per-event correction is\n    /// averaged across drained records and passed to\n    /// `band_table::render`, which displays it in an `adj mean`\n    /// column alongside the raw columns. See\n    /// `notes/overhead-model.md`.\n    pub fn report(\n        &mut self,\n        as_ticks: bool,\n        overhead: Option<&Overhead>,\n        decimals: Option<usize>,\n    ) {"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "## Band-table ns-mode shows 1 decimal (0.3.1)\n\nChanges `band_table::render` to format ns-mode cells with\none decimal place (`\"4.5 ns\"`) instead of rounding to\ninteger nanoseconds (`\"5 ns\"`). Ticks mode is unchanged —\ninteger counts are already precise. Resolves a long-\nstanding readability issue where sub-ns detail was\nsilently rounded away:\n\n- 1 hardware tick ≈ 0.26 ns on a 3.7 GHz box, so OoO-\n  coalesced `rdtsc` pairs and hdrhistogram's tightest\n  buckets (`range=1 tk`) rendered as `\"0 ns\"`, masking\n  real data.\n- Adjacent histogram buckets that hold distinct but\n  close values (e.g. 17 tk vs 18 tk at inner=1000)\n  collapsed to the same display (`\"5 ns\"`) despite being\n  resolvable in the underlying data.\n\nWith one decimal the distinction becomes visible:\n`\"4.5 ns\"` vs `\"4.7 ns\"` vs `\"5.0 ns\"` on the same hot-\ncluster region, and the p1-p10 OoO cluster that used to\nrender `0 ns` now shows `0.3 ns` — more honest.\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.0` → `0.3.1`.\n- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.3` → `0.1.4` — render\n  output format changed.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`: new local\n  `let decimals = if as_ticks { 0 } else { 1 };`\n  threaded through every `fmt_commas_f64` call in\n  `render()` (band rows, summary-mean/stdev strings,\n  trimmed-mean/stdev strings). Inline comment documents\n  the rationale. `cargo fmt` reformatted one\n  `hist_mean_adj_str` call site into multi-line form\n  since it crossed the width limit.\n- `README.md`: `## Usage` sample-output block regenerated\n  with the 1-decimal format.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **One decimal, not two.** Two decimals (`\"4.25 ns\"`)\n  surface slightly more detail but widen every ns cell by\n  another character, which crowds goal2's wide band-table\n  (values in the thousands of ns). One decimal is the\n  smallest change that resolves the \"1 tk → 0 ns\" masking\n  issue and keeps columns readable.\n- **Ticks mode stays integer.** Integer tick counts are\n  already exact — adding decimals would inject spurious\n  `\".0\"` noise without carrying any information. `-t` is\n  the right mode when sub-tick resolution matters\n  (there's no such thing as sub-tick).\n- **Single-step `0.3.1` patch release.** Mechanical,\n  local, single display concern — doesn't need the `-N`\n  ladder treatment. Follows `CLAUDE.md`'s \"single-step\n  recommended for mechanical changes\" guidance.",
  "new_string": "## Band-table decimals (default + `-d` override) (0.3.1)\n\nTwo related display tweaks to the band-table report, shipped\nas a single 0.3.1 patch:\n\n**Default**: `band_table::render` formats ns-mode cells with\none decimal place (`\"4.5 ns\"`) instead of rounding to integer\nnanoseconds (`\"5 ns\"`). Ticks mode is unchanged — integer\ncounts are already precise. Resolves a long-standing\nreadability issue where sub-ns detail was silently rounded\naway:\n\n- 1 hardware tick ≈ 0.26 ns on a 3.7 GHz box, so OoO-\n  coalesced `rdtsc` pairs and hdrhistogram's tightest\n  buckets (`range=1 tk`) rendered as `\"0 ns\"`, masking\n  real data.\n- Adjacent histogram buckets that hold distinct but close\n  values (e.g. 17 tk vs 18 tk at inner=1000) collapsed\n  to the same display (`\"5 ns\"`) despite being resolvable\n  in the underlying data.\n\nWith one decimal the distinction becomes visible:\n`\"4.5 ns\"` vs `\"4.7 ns\"` vs `\"5.0 ns\"` on the same hot-\ncluster region, and the p1-p10 OoO cluster that used to\nrender `0 ns` now shows `0.3 ns` — more honest.\n\n**Override**: new `-d / --decimals <N>` CLI flag on both\n`goal1` and `goal2`. Omit for the mode-aware default (0 for\nticks, 1 for ns); pass an integer to force that precision\nin either mode. `-d 2` surfaces hdrhistogram's full 0.1 %\nrelative resolution (`\"4.22 ns\"` vs `\"4.48 ns\"`); `-d 0`\nreproduces the pre-0.3.1 integer-ns look for anyone who\npreferred it.\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.0` → `0.3.1`.\n- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.3` → `0.1.4` — public\n  API: `TProbe::report` and `band_table::render` both gain\n  a `decimals: Option<usize>` parameter.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`:\n  `render(..., decimals: Option<usize>)`; top of the\n  function resolves `decimals.unwrap_or(if as_ticks { 0 }\n  else { 1 })` and that value threads through every\n  `fmt_commas_f64` call in band rows and summary rows.\n  Docstring updated.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/tprobe.rs`: `TProbe::report(&mut self,\n  as_ticks, overhead, decimals)`. Forwarded to\n  `band_table::render`. Test call sites updated (pass\n  `None` since they don't care about display precision).\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`,\n  `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: new\n  `#[arg(short = 'd', long)] decimals: Option<usize>`\n  field on each `Cli`. `report` calls forward\n  `cli.decimals`.\n- `README.md`: `## Usage` sample-output block regenerated\n  with the 1-decimal default. `### Flags` gains a\n  `-d / --decimals <N>` bullet between `-t / --ticks` and\n  `-p / --pin`.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **One decimal as the default, not zero.** Zero decimals\n  hid the sub-ns detail that the underlying histogram\n  already resolved; moving to one decimal surfaces it\n  with minimal column growth (+2 chars per cell). The\n  `-d` escape hatch handles the \"I want more / less\"\n  cases.\n- **Ticks mode default stays at 0 decimals.** Integer\n  tick counts are already exact — `\".0\"` would be\n  spurious noise. Users who want decimals in ticks mode\n  can explicitly pass `-d N`; the default won't inject\n  them.\n- **`decimals: Option<usize>` at the API layer, not a\n  `usize` with sentinel.** `None` means \"use the\n  mode-aware default\" and is the right abstraction for\n  the smart-default / override split. Callers who just\n  want the default pass `None` (and pay no attention to\n  what it resolves to); callers who care pass\n  `Some(n)`.\n- **`-d` on the CLI rather than two separate flags** (e.g.\n  `--precision` plus `--raw-integer`). One flag, one\n  integer, one abstraction — matches the pattern\n  already established by `-i/--inner`, `-w/--warmup`,\n  `-p/--pin`.\n- **Single-step `0.3.1` patch release.** Mechanical,\n  local, single display concern plus a small CLI flag —\n  doesn't need the `-N` ladder treatment."
}
```

> AGENT

All clean. Verified all three modes:

- **Default** (`goal1 0.3 -i 1000 -p 0`) — 1 decimal: `4.5 ns`, `5.0 ns`, hot-bucket split visible.
- **`-d 2`** — 2 decimals: `4.22 ns`, `4.48 ns`, `4.75 ns` — hdrhistogram's 0.1 % buckets fully exposed.
- **`-d 0`** — 0 decimals: integer ns, reproduces pre-0.3.1 look.

Proposed commit:

**Title**
```
feat: band-table decimals default + -d override (0.3.1)
```

**Body**
```
Two related display tweaks shipped together as 0.3.1:

1. ns-mode band-table cells now default to 1 decimal place
   ("4.5 ns") instead of integer ns ("5 ns"). Ticks mode
   stays integer — tick counts are already exact.
   Resolves a long-standing readability issue: 1 tk ≈
   0.26 ns on a 3.7 GHz box, so OoO-coalesced `rdtsc`
   pairs and tightest hdrhistogram buckets used to render
   as "0 ns", masking real data.

2. New `-d / --decimals <N>` CLI flag on goal1 / goal2.
   Omit for the mode-aware default (0 for ticks, 1 for
   ns); pass an integer to force that precision in either
   mode. `-d 2` surfaces hdrhistogram's full 0.1 %
   relative resolution; `-d 0` reproduces the pre-0.3.1
   integer-ns look.

- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.3 → 0.1.4` — API
  change.
- `crates/tprobe/src/band_table.rs`: `render` gains
  `decimals: Option<usize>`. Top of function resolves
  `decimals.unwrap_or(if as_ticks { 0 } else { 1 })`;
  that value threads through every `fmt_commas_f64`
  call.
- `crates/tprobe/src/tprobe.rs`: `TProbe::report`
  signature gains `decimals: Option<usize>`. Test call
  sites updated to pass `None`.
- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`,
  `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: new
  `#[arg(short = 'd', long)] decimals: Option<usize>`
  on each `Cli`; `report` calls forward `cli.decimals`.
- `README.md`: `## Usage` sample regenerated with
  1-decimal default; `### Flags` gains a `-d /
  --decimals <N>` bullet.
- `notes/chores-01.md`: new section with five design
  decisions (1 not 0 default; ticks stays 0; `Option`
  at API layer; single `-d` flag; single-step patch).
- `notes/todo.md`: Done entry + reference `[16]`.
```

OK to run `vc-x1 push main`?

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "- **Single-step `0.3.1` patch release.** Mechanical,\n  local, single display concern plus a small CLI flag —\n  doesn't need the `-N` ladder treatment.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness",
  "new_string": "- **Single-step `0.3.1` patch release.** Mechanical,\n  local, single display concern plus a small CLI flag —\n  doesn't need the `-N` ladder treatment.\n\n## Comma-format summary counts (0.3.2)\n\nPulls `fmt_commas` up into `tprobe`'s public API so the\n`goal1` / `goal2` binaries can reuse it on their summary\nlines. The raw message counts on those lines used to render\nundifferentiated — e.g. `goal1: 61687000 messages` — which\nat 8-9 digits was hard to read at a glance. They now read\n`goal1: 61,687,000 messages`, matching the band-table\nreport's existing comma formatting for `count=N` and band\n`count` columns.\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.1` → `0.3.2`.\n- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.4` → `0.1.5` — API\n  surface gains two re-exports.\n- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: `pub(crate) mod fmt;` →\n  `pub mod fmt;`; adds `pub use fmt::{fmt_commas,\n  fmt_commas_f64};` at the crate root.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`,\n  `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: `use tprobe::...`\n  gains `fmt_commas`; the `goalN: <count> messages ...`\n  summary line uses `fmt_commas(count)`. goal2 also\n  comma-formats the per-actor `\"  actor i: handled N\n  messages\"` line.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **Promote `fmt_commas` to public rather than duplicate\n  it.** The utility already lives in `tprobe` (needed by\n  the band-table renderer). Binaries reusing it via\n  `pub use` is cheaper than copy-pasting two functions\n  into actor-x1 — and if `tprobe` ever publishes, the\n  helpers travel with it as part of a coherent surface.\n- **Re-export at the crate root, keep `fmt` module also\n  public.** `use tprobe::fmt_commas` is the ergonomic\n  short path; `use tprobe::fmt::fmt_commas` still works\n  for callers who prefer module-qualified imports.\n  Exporting only `fmt_commas` would be stricter but the\n  paired `fmt_commas_f64` has the same utility shape,\n  so both go. `fmt::fmt` module name is workspace-local\n  and unlikely to collide.\n- **Only message counts comma-formatted, not the\n  throughput / wall-clock floats.** `204.486 M msg/s` is\n  fine as a 4-5 char decimal; `0.300s` is likewise\n  small. The comma-format helper targets integer counts\n  where the digit grouping actually helps.\n- **Single-step `0.3.2` patch release.** Two-file\n  application change plus a one-line `lib.rs` re-export.\n  No `-N` ladder.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"
}

Proposed commit:

**Title**
```
feat: comma-format summary message counts (0.3.2)
```

**Body**
```
Reuses `tprobe`'s `fmt_commas` on the `goal1` / `goal2`
summary lines and on goal2's per-actor count line. The
raw counts at 6–9 digits read poorly (`61687000`,
`104092`, `52046`); comma-grouped they scan instantly
(`61,687,000`, `104,092`, `52,046`), matching the
band-table's existing `count=N` and per-band `count`
formatting.

- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.4 → 0.1.5` — two new
  re-exports at the crate root.
- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: `pub(crate) mod fmt;` →
  `pub mod fmt;`; adds `pub use fmt::{fmt_commas,
  fmt_commas_f64};` so binaries can reach them via
  `use tprobe::fmt_commas`.
- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`,
  `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: import
  `fmt_commas` from tprobe; wrap the message-count
  placeholder on the summary line. goal2 also wraps the
  per-actor `"  actor i: handled N messages"` line.
- `notes/chores-01.md`: new chore section with four
  design decisions (promote not duplicate; export at
  crate root; counts only not floats; single-step).
- `notes/todo.md`: Done entry + reference `[17]`.
```

> DEVELOPER

How would we determine the "precision" we are able to measure

> AGENT

Precision of a tprobe measurement is the product of several floor sources. Worth separating the sources from the measurement technique.

**The floors (smallest things we can discriminate):**

- **Hardware tick resolution.** 1 TSC tick ≈ 0.27 ns on a 3.7 GHz box. Nothing below this exists.
- **Apparatus framing noise.** Calibration fits `framing_ticks` with some noise — we've observed 21 → 29 tick swings run-to-run (~2 ns on this box). That noise directly dirties `adj mean`.
- **OoO rdtsc coalescing.** Back-to-back `rdtsc` pairs with nothing serializing between them can measure 0–1 ticks even when real work happened; the p1-p10 OoO cluster at inner=1 is this.
- **hdrhistogram bucket width.** 3 sig figs = 0.1 % relative. At 20 tk, bucket width ≈ 1 tk; at 2,000 tk, ≈ 2 tk. Below ~1,000 tk, quantization is essentially exact.
- **Unmeasurable systemic noise.** Cache misses, context switches, interrupts. Shows up in `p99-max` tail; handled by preferring `mean min-p99` for A/B work.

**The empirical technique (and what we're missing today):**

For a given configuration the right measurement is the *run-to-run* stdev of `mean min-p99`. Something like:

```
for _ in 1..=30; do goal1 2 -i 1000 -p 0 >> runs.txt; done
# parse out mean min-p99, compute sample stdev
```

The single-run `stdev min-p99` tells us within-run noise; the cross-run stdev tells us the measurement's repeatability. Those aren't the same number — pinning typically tightens within-run noise more than cross-run noise. A/B-significant difference is roughly `2 × cross_run_stdev`.

On your 3900X I'd expect:
- goal1 `-i 1000`: cross-run stdev ~0.2–0.5 ns on mean min-p99 (≈10% of the signal).
- goal1 `-i 1`: cross-run stdev ~1–2 ns (adj mean picks up the calibration-framing swing).
- goal2 pinned same-CCX: cross-run stdev ~30–100 tk on mean min-p99 (≈2–5%).

**What we could build (if worth it):**

A `--repeat N` flag that runs the full measurement N times internally, reports per-run summary lines plus a cross-run stdev/mean on `mean min-p99` (and `adj mean min-p99`). Single command, right number for A/B. Small feature — ~30–50 lines across the binaries, no probe-API change needed since it just loops the existing `run(warmup, measurement)` sequence.

Want me to add it as a todo, or scope it for a 0.3.3 / 0.4.0 step?

That noise\n  propagates directly to `adj mean` since framing is the\n  bulk of the per-event correction at small `inner`. See\n  `crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md` \"Calibration\n  preconditions\" and \"Framing\".\n- **OoO `rdtsc` coalescing.** Back-to-back `rdtsc`\n  pairs with nothing serializing between them can\n  measure 0–1 ticks even when real work happened; the\n  p1-p10 cluster at `inner=1` is exactly this. Not a\n  bug — the probe reports what the hardware reported.\n- **hdrhistogram bucket width.** 3 sig figs = 0.1 %\n  relative precision. At 20 tk, bucket width ≈ 1 tk;\n  at 2,000 tk, ≈ 2 tk. Below ~1,000 tk, quantization is\n  essentially exact.\n- **Unmeasurable systemic noise.** Cache misses,\n  context switches, interrupts (see the\n  \"unmeasurable overhead\" section of\n  `crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`). Shows up in\n  `p99-max` tail; handled in practice by preferring\n  `mean min-p99` for A/B work.\n\n### Technique — measuring repeatability empirically\n\nFor a given configuration, the right number is the\n*run-to-run* stdev of `mean min-p99` (and/or\n`adj mean min-p99`). Shell approximation without a\nbuilt-in flag:\n\n```sh\nfor _ in $(seq 1 30); do goal1 2 -i 1000 -p 0 >> runs.txt; done\n# extract `mean min-p99` from each report, compute sample stdev\n```\n\nThe single-run `stdev min-p99` tells us *within-run*\nnoise; the *cross-run* stdev tells us the measurement's\nrepeatability. They aren't the same number — pinning\ntypically tightens within-run more than cross-run. The\nbot thinks a useful heuristic for A/B work is:\n**a difference smaller than `2 × cross-run stdev` is\nprobably not real.**\n\n### Expected-scale heuristics (3900X, ~3.7 GHz)\n\nThe bot thinks the typical ranges on this box are:\n\n- goal1 `-i 1000`: cross-run stdev ~0.2–0.5 ns on\n  `mean min-p99` (~10 % of the signal).\n- goal1 `-i 1`: cross-run stdev ~1–2 ns on\n  `adj mean min-p99` — picks up the calibration-framing\n  swing directly.\n- goal2 pinned same-CCX (e.g. `--pin 1,12`): cross-run\n  stdev ~30–100 tk on `mean min-p99` (~2–5 %).\n- goal2 unpinned: cross-run stdev much higher (20–30 %\n  swings observed across 1 s vs 20 s runs before\n  pinning was applied).\n\nHardware-dependent numbers; compare against your own\nbaseline, not these.\n\n### Future `--repeat N` flag (tracked as todo `[18]`)\n\nThe bot thinks a built-in repeatability measurement\nwould be worth having: a `--repeat N` flag on `goal1`\nand `goal2` that runs the full warmup+measurement\nsequence `N` times, aggregates per-run summary lines,\nand prints cross-run mean and stdev on `mean min-p99`\nand `adj mean min-p99`. Single command, right number\nfor A/B — replaces the shell loop above and its\none-off parsing. The bot thinks ~30–50 lines across\nthe two binaries, no probe-API change (just loops the\nexisting `run(warmup, measurement)` / `run_for` calls\nand accumulates their reports).\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

hmm, should we put this in a tprobe/notes/chores?

> AGENT

Good question. The content is squarely tprobe-specific (measurement limits of the probe), so it logically belongs in `crates/tprobe/notes/`, not workspace-level chores. A few shapes to consider:

**(a) Move it to `crates/tprobe/notes/precision.md` as a standalone reference doc.**

Natural peer to `overhead-model.md` and `design.md`. The content is reference methodology, not a completed chore or release step, so a plain topical filename fits better than a "chores" file. Matches how we organized at `0.3.0-1`.

**(b) Fold into `crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`** as a new "Precision and repeatability" section.

The floors analysis connects directly to overhead (framing noise, unmeasurable-noise bucket are already covered there). Downside: the empirical measurement technique and the `--repeat N` proposal drift a bit from "overhead model" — they're about how to measure the probe, not what overhead is.

**(c) Start a `crates/tprobe/notes/chores-01.md` scoped to tprobe.**

Parallel to the workspace `/notes/chores-01.md`. Opens a larger question — should tprobe have its own release-chronology file separate from the workspace's? Today tprobe bumps (0.1.1 → 0.1.5) ride alongside actor-x1's release chronology in the workspace chores file. Splitting them would be more honest if tprobe is genuinely a separate crate with its own history, but also duplicates the per-release narrative that already threads both crates together. The bot thinks that split is worth doing *at the same time* as the repo split — not before.

My lean: **(a)** — standalone reference doc at `crates/tprobe/notes/precision.md`. Clean fit with the existing `notes/design.md` / `notes/overhead-model.md` pattern, and if tprobe splits later it travels along. Chore section in the workspace chores-01.md that captured the analysis stays as the record of *when the analysis was added*, but just points at `crates/tprobe/notes/precision.md` rather than containing the content itself.

Proceed with (a), or want (b) / (c)?

> DEVELOPER

(a) we can move it later if desired

  "content": "# Measurement precision and repeatability\n\nCaptures the precision floors a `tprobe` measurement is\nbound by, the technique for determining empirical\nrepeatability across runs, and expected-scale heuristics\nobserved on this workspace's development hardware.\n\n## Floors — the smallest things we can discriminate\n\n- **Hardware tick resolution.** 1 TSC tick ≈ 0.27 ns on a\n  3.7 GHz box. Nothing below this exists; it's the physical\n  lower bound on discriminable time.\n- **Apparatus framing noise.** Calibration fits\n  `framing_ticks` with run-to-run noise — observed 21 → 29\n  tick swings on the same box (~2 ns). That noise\n  propagates directly to `adj mean` since framing is the\n  bulk of the per-event correction at small `inner`. See\n  [`overhead-model.md`](overhead-model.md)'s \"Calibration\n  preconditions\" and \"Framing\" subsections.\n- **OoO `rdtsc` coalescing.** Back-to-back `rdtsc` pairs\n  with nothing serializing between them can measure 0–1\n  ticks even when real work happened; the p1-p10 cluster\n  at `inner=1` is exactly this. Not a bug — the probe\n  reports what the hardware reported.\n- **hdrhistogram bucket width.** 3 sig figs = 0.1 %\n  relative precision. At 20 tk, bucket width ≈ 1 tk; at\n  2,000 tk, ≈ 2 tk. Below ~1,000 tk, quantization is\n  essentially exact.\n- **Unmeasurable systemic noise.** Cache misses, context\n  switches, interrupts (see\n  [`overhead-model.md`](overhead-model.md)'s \"Unmeasurable\n  overhead\" section). Shows up in the `p99-max` tail;\n  handled in practice by preferring `mean min-p99` for A/B\n  work.\n\n## Technique — measuring repeatability empirically\n\nFor a given configuration, the right number is the\n*run-to-run* stdev of `mean min-p99` (and/or\n`adj mean min-p99`). Shell approximation without a built-in\nflag:\n\n```sh\nfor _ in $(seq 1 30); do\n    goal1 2 -i 1000 -p 0 >> runs.txt\ndone\n# extract `mean min-p99` from each report, compute sample stdev\n```\n\nThe single-run `stdev min-p99` tells us *within-run* noise;\nthe *cross-run* stdev tells us the measurement's\nrepeatability. They aren't the same number — pinning\ntypically tightens within-run more than cross-run. The bot\nthinks a useful heuristic for A/B work is:\n**a difference smaller than `2 × cross-run stdev` is\nprobably not real.**\n\n## Expected-scale heuristics (3900X, ~3.7 GHz)\n\nThe bot thinks the typical ranges on this box are:\n\n- goal1 `-i 1000`: cross-run stdev ~0.2–0.5 ns on\n  `mean min-p99` (~10 % of the signal).\n- goal1 `-i 1`: cross-run stdev ~1–2 ns on\n  `adj mean min-p99` — picks up the calibration-framing\n  swing directly.\n- goal2 pinned same-CCX (e.g. `--pin 1,12`): cross-run\n  stdev ~30–100 tk on `mean min-p99` (~2–5 %).\n- goal2 unpinned: cross-run stdev much higher (20–30 %\n  swings observed across 1 s vs 20 s runs before pinning\n  was applied).\n\nHardware-dependent numbers; compare against your own\nbaseline, not these.\n\n## Future `--repeat N` flag\n\nThe bot thinks a built-in repeatability measurement would\nbe worth having: a `--repeat N` flag on `goal1` and `goal2`\nthat runs the full warmup+measurement sequence `N` times,\naggregates per-run summary lines, and prints cross-run mean\nand stdev on `mean min-p99` and `adj mean min-p99`. Single\ncommand, right number for A/B — replaces the shell loop\nabove and its one-off parsing. The bot thinks ~30–50 lines\nacross the two binaries, no probe-API change (just loops\nthe existing `run(warmup, measurement)` / `run_for` calls\nand accumulates their reports).\n\nTracked in the workspace `notes/todo.md`.\n"
}

estimate effor doing goal1 and 2 with criterion

> AGENT

**Estimate: 3–5 focused hours of work, single-step 0.4.0 probably fits, though a `-N` ladder also works if you'd like each piece reviewable on its own.**

**Breakdown:**

| Step | Effort | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Wire criterion as dev-dep | 5 min | `dev-dependencies` entry + `[[bench]]` in `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`, `harness = false`. |
| Expose a benchmarkable API | 30–60 min | Add `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch(inner) -> u64` (one batch, no deadline check). `run_for` with a duration doesn't fit criterion's "call this closure repeatedly" shape. |
| goal1 bench | 30 min | `benches/goal1.rs`: set up runtime once, seed, loop `b.iter(|| rt.dispatch_batch(1000))`. Straightforward. |
| goal2 bench | 45–60 min | Trickier — criterion's default `b.iter` would spin threads per-iteration (setup cost dominates). Use `iter_custom` to keep threads alive and measure one ping-pong round-trip as the iteration. Need a `MultiThreadRuntime::dispatch_one()` or similar that sends+receives one message per call. |
| Cross-validation narrative | 30–60 min | A short `crates/tprobe/notes/cross-validation.md` or `benches/README.md` comparing criterion's estimate to tprobe's `mean min-p99` for the same workload. The bot thinks they should agree within ~10 % on goal1 and ~15 % on goal2. Big divergence would be interesting (and warrant investigation of probe bias). |
| Polish — docs, README entry | 30 min | Mention under Usage or a new Benches section; note how to run (`cargo bench`). |

**Open design questions (will affect effort):**

1. **Shared benchmarkable API.** Adding `dispatch_batch` is a tprobe-agnostic public API addition to `actor-x1`. The bot thinks this is fine — it's a natural primitive the goal1 CLI already uses internally — but it's an API decision, not a pure refactor. Similar for goal2. If you'd rather keep the current API closed and fudge the bench against `run_for(tiny_duration, inner)`, it's hackier but ~15 min faster.

2. **Measurement target.** Criterion defaults to "time per call"; our interesting number is "time per message". That's just `/1000` for goal1 and `/1` for goal2, easy. Criterion also supports `throughput(Throughput::Elements(N))` which reports in messages/sec directly — the bot thinks using that makes the output immediately comparable to goal1's existing `M msg/s` line.

3. **goal2's warmup story.** Criterion runs its own warmup automatically; tprobe's app-level warmup is irrelevant here. But *calibration* isn't needed either for criterion — we're not trying to subtract framing, just measuring wall-clock elapsed. So `criterion` benches skip `calibrate()` entirely, which simplifies things.

**Multi-step ladder shape (if you want it):**

- `0.4.0-0` — plan marker.
- `0.4.0-1` — expose `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch` + `MultiThreadRuntime::dispatch_one` (or equivalent).
- `0.4.0-2` — goal1 bench.
- `0.4.0-3` — goal2 bench.
- `0.4.0-4` — cross-validation narrative in `crates/tprobe/notes/`.
- `0.4.0` — close, README refresh.

The bot's lean: single-step `0.4.0` if you're confident about the `dispatch_batch` shape; multi-step if the API pieces deserve separate review. Either way, no probe-side changes — just benchmarks exercising the existing public surface.
